# PROGRESS

Durable memory of the phiOS step loop. One row per step from `phios-agent-brief.md` §6
(the main-line backlog). This file is updated in the same commit as the step that
changes a row's status — never in a separate commit.

## Status legend

- `todo` — not started.
- `awaiting-verification` — the agent has written, committed, and pushed to `github`;
  waiting on the user to run the step's `VERIFY` block and confirm `DONE WHEN`.
- `verified` — the user has confirmed `DONE WHEN`. The next step's commit is what
  moves a row from `awaiting-verification` to `verified` and fills in the `Commit`
  column, because a commit cannot record its own hash in advance.
- `blocked` — cannot proceed; see `Note` for what is missing and who owns closing it.

| S-06 | razer input diagnostics | verified | | 2026-09-07 | Diagnosis only, depends on S-04 (already verified). Nothing written to the repo; the deliverable was the diagnostic command set, executed by the user over several rounds (`/proc/bus/input/devices`, `libinput debug-events`, targeted `evtest` per device, `lsusb`, `ls /sys/bus/iio/devices/`). The internal keyboard is one USB composite device (`lsusb`: `1532:026a` `Razer USA, Ltd Razer Blade`, matching the vendor id `bin/phios-capabilities` already probes for Chroma) exposing three evdev nodes: `event20` (interface 0, `cap:k`, has the LEDs; carries ordinary alphanumeric typing and the Fn key's own synthesized combos), `event8` (interface 1, `cap:kp`; carries both the bare F-row and every Fn-held media/brightness function), `event9` (interface 2, mouse-like, not exercised — likely the touchpad's supplementary buttons). Fn itself emits no independent Linux event at all, confirmed by a dedicated 5-second hold producing nothing on any node: it is a pure hardware-level modifier inside the keyboard's own firmware that changes which HID report the F-row sends, and the same firmware also switches the backlight to a function-only indicator and (per the user) drives the keyboard-illumination keys `Fn+F10`/`F11` with no OS-visible event either — consistent with Fn's behaviour throughout. Every key `funzionalita §2.15` calls broken is already reported by the kernel with standard, correctly-named USB HID scancodes, so no `hwdb` fix is needed for any of them and the real failure is above the driver, almost certainly the absence of any compositor keybinding yet (`S-38`/`S-43`, not built at this stage of the project): bare `F1`/`F2`/`F3`/`F4`/`F8`/`F9` on `event8` → literal `KEY_F1` (`0x7003a`) / `KEY_F2` (`0x7003b`) / `KEY_F3` (`0x7003c`) / `KEY_F4` (`0x7003d`) / `KEY_F8` (`0x70041`) / `KEY_F9` (`0x70042`); `Fn+F1` → `KEY_MUTE` (`0xc00e2`); `Fn+F2` → `KEY_VOLUMEDOWN` (`0xc00ea`); `Fn+F3` → `KEY_VOLUMEUP` (`0xc00e9`); `Fn+F8` → `KEY_BRIGHTNESSDOWN` (`0xc0070`); `Fn+F9` → `KEY_BRIGHTNESSUP` (`0xc006f`). Also captured at the user's request, beyond this step's stated goal but the same conclusion: `Fn+F5`/`F6`/`F7` → `KEY_PREVIOUSSONG`/`KEY_PLAYPAUSE`/`KEY_NEXTSONG` (`0xc00b6`/`0xc00cd`/`0xc00b5`). `Fn+F4` is the one exception and is not a function/media code at all: the firmware synthesizes the literal Windows shortcut Super+P on `event20` (`KEY_RIGHTMETA` held, scancode `0x700e7`, the standard HID Right-GUI usage, plus `KEY_P` tapped underneath, scancode `0x70013`) — a hardware-level stand-in for Windows' display-projection panel, not a missing keycode; an equivalent on Linux needs a compositor bind on Super+P (`S-38`), not an `hwdb` rule, and may need to dodge a clash if Super ends up the primary window-management modifier. `Q-F01` closes definitively: `ls /sys/bus/iio/devices/` lists exactly one device, `iio:device0`, and its `name` reads `als` — a real ambient-light sensor is present, named exactly what `bin/phios-capabilities`'s existing heuristic already matches on, unblocking `S-42`'s True Tone (master plan, ambient colour-balance note). `DONE WHEN` met: every code `funzionalita §2.15` names is known; the `hwdb` rule itself, the `Fn+F4`/Super+P question, and the still-open `Q-F04`/`Q-F06` Chroma questions are left for `S-46` |

| S-10 | [BLOCKING] phi repository skeleton and --version | verified | 8517d84 | 2026-09-07 | First step to land in a repository other than this one, so its own commit hash above is a `phi` hash, not a `phios-dotfiles` one. `phi` is a dependency-free Go module (`go.mod` names the module `phi`, not a URL, since it is never imported by another module): `cmd/phi` is the entry point, `internal/cli` parses arguments, does TTY detection (a coarse `os.ModeCharDevice` check with no third-party dependency — it also reports `/dev/null` as a terminal, harmless today since styled only adds the Φ mark, but it will need a real `ioctl`-based check before any verb emits colour), and dispatches to a verb or falls back to `phi-<verb>` on `PATH` (ADR 017, exercised off-machine against a throwaway `phi-hello` script: arguments, stdin, and exit code all forward correctly). `internal/view` holds `help`/`--version`/`completion zsh` as pure string functions with no knowledge of terminals or process globals, kept separate from `internal/cli`'s dispatch logic from the first line per architettura §10.1.1. `internal/build` carries the version string (`"dev"` locally), overridable at link time via `-ldflags` for phi-packages's PKGBUILD (S-11) to set from the package version. Commands implemented: `phi --version`, `phi help`, `phi completion zsh`; bare `phi` and an unknown verb both print help/an error to stderr and exit 1. The help header carries the Φ mark (`U+03A6`, confirmed byte-for-byte, not one of the lowercase forms) in Role A — Tier 0, monochrome, static — only when stdout is a real terminal; redirected output drops it, the only "structured" behaviour this step exercises, since none of the three commands yet emits data a script would parse — the real plain-columns-vs-JSON decision waits for the first verb that does (S-13 `phi state list`, S-14 `phi doctor`). Off-machine: `go build`, `go vet` and `gofmt -l` clean; native (macOS) build run directly (`phi --version` → `phi dev`, help/no-args/unknown-verb/missing-completion-shell exit codes all correct, styled Φ mark confirmed under a pty via `script -q`, dropped under a pipe and under `> /dev/null`); a `GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64` cross-build also succeeded and produced a static ELF binary, confirmed with `file`, but was not executed there — no Linux host was available in that session. **Verified on real hardware since**: `razer` — `git clone` over `https` from the public `github` mirror (no credentials needed for read), `go build -o phi ./cmd/phi` silent/clean, `./phi --version` → `phi dev`, `./phi help` shows the styled `Φ phi dev` header on a real terminal, and the `phi-hello` PATH-fallback test forwards correctly. `DONE WHEN` met: the binary builds and the dispatcher falls back to PATH for unknown verbs, confirmed off-machine and on `razer` |
| S-11 | [BLOCKING] phi-packages and the [phi] pacman repository | verified | phi-packages 0876454..4c951d9 (4 commits) · dotfiles c5b0cb9..7f8e01f (3 commits) | 2026-09-07 | New repository `phi-packages` (`0876454` skeleton, `52952fe` a same-day key-consistency fixup — a second commit rather than an amend because this environment correctly refuses to force-push an already-pushed branch — and `1791f4c` adding `git` to `makedepends`, found on real hardware: `zotac`'s first `scripts/build phi` failed inside the chroot with "Cannot find the git package needed to handle git sources", because `source=()` is a `git+` URL but only `go` was declared, so a fresh `mkarchroot base-devel` chroot had no way to fetch it — all `phi-packages` hashes, same pattern S-10 set for landing outside `phios-dotfiles`): `phi/PKGBUILD` builds the CLI from a pinned git tag (`source=(...#tag=v${pkgver})`, never `latest`/`-git`), `scripts/build` wraps `mkarchroot`/`makechrootpkg -c` for a clean-chroot build on `zotac`, `scripts/publish` detached-signs a built package and runs `repo-add -s` into a local repo directory — never touches `mini`, since that requires an address this repository may never hold (`CLAUDE.md` rule 5); it requires `GPGKEY` explicitly rather than trusting gpg's default identity, since `zotac`'s user almost certainly already has an unrelated personal key and a silent mismatch between the key signing the packages and the key `repo-add -s` uses for the database would fail every install under `SigLevel = Required` with no obvious cause. `README.md` documents naming (`phi`, `phi-<component>`), versioning (hand-bumped `pkgver` pinned to a real tag; `pkgrel` for packaging-only rebuilds), where the repo database ends up, and host registration. Tagged and pushed `v0.1.0` on the `phi` repo, at the S-10 commit `8517d84` — no functional change, the tag exists only so `source=()` has something real to clone; `pkgver=0.1.0` in the PKGBUILD names exactly this tag. The exact `go build -trimpath -ldflags "-s -w -X phi/internal/build.Version=${pkgver}"` line from `build()` was run natively off-machine and confirmed `phi 0.1.0`; the chroot build itself was not run anywhere, no Linux host available. On the dotfiles side: `profiles/base/system/etc/pacman.d/phi-mirrorlist` (real, diffable path, confirmed via `--system-diff` on all three hosts) carries the `Server =` line with `<mini-overlay-address>` and `<port>` left as literal placeholders; `profiles/base/manual.txt` gained the two-line `[phi]`/`Include=` stanza for `/etc/pacman.conf` (appended as a single `grep -q` guarded `sudo sh -c 'printf ... >> ...'` line, not a heredoc, because `phios_read_list` truncates every line at its own `#` — confirmed by simulating the parser against the edited file; the `grep` guard makes the append idempotent, since unlike the multilib `sed` next to it in `profiles/gaming/manual.txt`, a plain append is not) and the `pacman-key --add`/`--lsign-key` step. **Deviation from the step card's literal wording, flagged for cheap veto:** the AGENT bullet and `phios-user-runbook.md` §5.4 both say the fragment lives at `profiles/*/system/pacman/`, but `bin/lib/system.sh` (`phios_system_collect`) mirrors the exact absolute path after `.../system/`, so a file at `system/pacman/x` would diff forever against the nonexistent `/pacman/x` — the runbook's own instruction to read it with `--system-diff` is only satisfiable at a real path, so `system/etc/pacman.d/phi-mirrorlist` was used instead; the runbook still names the stale path and was not edited (out of scope for an agent that doesn't own that document). **Second deviation, same flag:** the step card's USER section names only `zotac` and `razer` for registration, but master plan §3.1 ("registrato in `/etc/pacman.conf` di ogni macchina") and runbook §5.2 step 2 and §5.4's own header both say every machine, so the fragment and manual step were placed in `profiles/base` (all three hosts) rather than `profiles/desktop` (`zotac`+`razer` only) — master plan wins conflicts per `CLAUDE.md`. Not written in this step, deliberately: the darkhttpd systemd unit that serves `/srv/pkg/phi` — the step card's USER section has the user set that up by hand, and the AGENT section names no unit file. `--dry-run`/`--check` remain unusable off-machine, same pre-existing BSD-`realpath` failure `S-05` already recorded, unrelated to this step; verified with `--system-diff` instead, on all three hosts, off-machine only. **Verified on real hardware, all three hosts.** `zotac`: full loop exercised end to end — toolchain install (`pacman -Sy` hit a transient 404 on one mirror for an unrelated `devtools` dependency, resolved by pacman's own mirror fallback, unrelated to this step), key generation, `scripts/build phi` (succeeded on the second attempt, after the `git`-in-`makedepends` fix above), `scripts/publish` (produced a signed `phi.db.tar.zst`; `makepkg` also emitted the standard `phi-debug` split package, never published — `scripts/publish` was only given the main package, which is sufficient), `pacman-key --add`/`--lsign-key`, `pacman -Sy`, `pacman -S phi`, `phi --version` → `phi 0.1.0`. Getting the built repo onto `mini` needed two more fixes beyond anything either repository declares: `rsync ~/phi-repo/ mini:/srv/pkg/phi/` first failed because `/srv/pkg/phi` didn't exist yet, then failed again on `chgrp`/symlink creation until the directory's ownership matched the transferring user — exactly the `mkdir`/`chown` sequence `phios-user-runbook.md` §5.3 already documents, so nothing here needed to change, only doing that step before the transfer. `razer` and `mini`: `pacman-key --add` using the public key copied over per the README fix above, the `phi-mirrorlist` `Server` line written directly rather than by `sed` per the same fix, the `[phi]` stanza appended, `pacman -Sy` picked up and installed `phi` cleanly on both — confirmed by the user. `DONE WHEN` met and exceeded: `phi --version` runs, installed by `pacman`, on all three hosts, not just the two the step card's USER section named — satisfying master plan §3.1's "ogni macchina" the way the `base`-profile placement decision above anticipated. No IP address, hostname, key fingerprint, or private key material was written into any repository at any point in this step — checked against the working tree and the full history of all three repositories before this commit |

Both now distinguish a real command error from pacman/systemd's own normal "nothing to report" exit, reported `Unknown` for the former; reasoned from pacman/systemctl's well-established exit-code conventions (the same convention `bin/lib/packages.sh` already relies on for `pacman -T`) rather than a captured real transcript, since neither failure mode has actually occurred on any host yet. Confirmed against the user's own pasted transcripts: `sudo smartctl -H /dev/sda` printed the real assessment line, "SMART overall-health self-assessment test result: PASSED"; the same command without `sudo` printed only the permission-denied line, no assessment line at all. Fixed in `c1fa74e` (tagged `v0.4.1`): `assessmentVerdict` now looks for that one specific self-assessment line instead of scanning the whole buffer for a bare substring, so an unrelated error can never again be read as a health verdict; the fallback path now surfaces smartctl's own error line, with a "(needs root)" hint, instead of a generic exit-status message. The same review's `lsblk` output also showed `dm-0` is mini's `/srv` LUKS mapping (named `data`), not a physical disk, so `dm`/`md` were added to the `/sys/block` exclusion list — it was only ever noise (`could not read`), never a false positive, but never belonged in the sweep either. Verified the fix against the exact real transcripts via a throwaway in-package test, not committed. **Separately confirmed, not a bug:** `smartctl not available` on `razer`/`zotac` is correct and by design — `smartmontools` is declared in no profile's `packages.txt` anywhere in this repository (grep across all profiles: zero matches; 85%/95% warn/fail thresholds are this step's own judgment call, no planning document sets one, flagged for cheap veto), failed systemd units (`systemctl --failed`, system and user scope — `bin/lib/system.sh`'s `phios_services_report` explicitly never queries systemd, "that gap does not close, at S-05 or ever"; that boundary is the installer's, not `phi doctor`'s — asking systemd how it's doing is this command's entire job), dotfiles drift (shells to `bin/phios-install --check` rather than reimplementing profile/package/file planning a second time in Go; maps its documented exit codes 0/1/2 to ok/PROBLEM/unknown), SMART status (enumerates real disks from `/sys/block`, excluding `loop`/`ram`/`zram`/`sr`, runs `smartctl -H` on each, parses the self-assessment line as text since `smartctl(8)`'s exit-code bit layout was not reachable to confirm, same gap S-05 flagged for `smartd.conf`'s `-m` token), service status (reads `hosts/<host>.txt` and `profiles/*/services-*.txt` — the read side of the boundary `phios_services_report` only ever prints — and checks each declared unit's actual `systemctl` state; `inactive` is not flagged, since `services-user.txt`'s own comment documents Arch never auto-enabling user units on install, only `failed` is), and package categories (`pacman -Qm`, foreign packages, against `CLAUDE.md` rule 6 — T0 + phi-packages only, `Q-01` deferred — so any non-empty result is exactly the AUR/T4 violation that rule forbids; phi-packages vs. other T0 is bucketed by name, `phi`/`phi-*`, a heuristic since pacman has no post-install "which repo did this come from" query, flagged the same way S-04 flagged its device-name heuristics). On `mini` only, an added `/srv` mount check compares the device ids of `/` and `/srv` rather than parsing mount tables; `PROBLEM` when they match (the LUKS volume is still locked — expected right after every reboot per master plan §9.2, but still worth a timer's attention if it stays that way). Every check degrades to `unknown` rather than guessing when its tool or privilege is unavailable — never silently omitted, never promoted to a false `ok` — and `Report.ExitCode` is non-zero exactly on a `PROBLEM`, never on `unknown`, so an environment limit (no `pacman` here, no `/sys/block` here) can never fabricate a red result; `tokens.Root()` failing no longer aborts the whole command the way `phi theme`'s does — dotfiles drift and service status report that failure through themselves, and disk/systemd/SMART/packages still run, since they need no checkout. Confirmed off-machine: `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l` clean; `phi doctor` runs end to end and the real `statfs` reading on this machine correctly produced a `PROBLEM` at 97.5% used (not a fixture); every other check correctly reported `unknown` for the right reason (no `systemctl`/`smartctl`/`pacman`/`/sys/block` here, no `hosts/<this-hostname>.txt` in the repo). The profile-resolution path specifically was verified against a real declared host by symlinking `profiles/`, `bin/` and `design/` into a scratch tree with `hosts/` pointed at `zotac.txt`'s real content, run via a throwaway `cmd/_s14check` deleted immediately after (confirmed by `git status` showing no trace): with a real host recognized, service status walked past profile resolution and correctly enumerated `zotac`'s declared units, stopping only on the expected "systemctl not available" here. New repository `phi-shell` (first commit `5732e8f` — following S-10's precedent, its own hash is a `phi-shell` hash, not a `phios-dotfiles` one). Scope is exactly the S-20 AGENT bullets, not the full master plan §8.2 tree: `shell.qml` plus the four `Config/` singletons. `Widgets/`, `Services/`, `Bar/`, `Panels/`, `Launcher/` and the rest land with the steps that own them (S-21 onward) — stubbing empty directories for them now would be scope creep git cannot even track. `shell.qml` (`ShellRoot`) instantiates one non-visual `QtObject` delegate per `Quickshell.screens` entry via `Variants` (ADR 077, "designed for N monitors from day one") — deliberately no `PanelWindow` or anything visible, since `DONE WHEN` requires "produces no surface yet"; the first real surface is the bar (S-22). `Config/Tokens.qml` is generated by `phi theme set` (S-12) from a new template this step adds to `phios-dotfiles` (see below) and is gitignored; `Config/Tokens.example.qml` is a checked-in, hand-rendered snapshot of the current dark variant for anyone who has not run `phi theme set` yet — confirmed **not** hand-typed-and-hoped: built the actual `phi` binary from this checkout and ran `phi theme render`/`phi theme set` for real against the new template, `diff`'d the output against `Tokens.example.qml`, byte-identical apart from the two files' intentionally different header comments (one describes a generated file, the other an unmaintained example) — the property values and their order match exactly. `Config/Appearance.qml` exposes semantic roles derived from `Tokens` (structure, accent, semantic tiers, typography, size scale, spacing, shape, z-layers, motion) as proper QML types (`color`/`real`/`int`), converting each token's stored string ("120ms", "14px") itself — `design/README.md`'s own contract ("a consumer that needs a bare number strips the unit"). Tier 3 (syntax), the ANSI 16, selection and terminal-cursor tokens are deliberately **not** exposed here: they are terminal-emulator concepts and no surface in master plan §8.3's inventory needs them; they stay reachable on `Tokens` directly until a real consumer asks, rather than inventing exposure surface nothing uses yet. **A real correctness catch, not a hypothetical one:** `PHI_OVERLAY_SCRIM` is stored `#rrggbbaa` (CSS order, alpha last, per `tokens.dark.sh`'s own comment) but QML's native 8-digit colour string parsing is `#aarrggbb` (Qt order, alpha first) — assigning the token string directly to a `property color` would have silently produced a transparent-blue scrim instead of translucent black. `Appearance._color()` parses the four channels by hand and builds the colour with `Qt.rgba()` instead, applied uniformly to every colour role so there is one code path, not an exception carved out for the one token that would have broken. `Config/Capabilities.qml` shells out to `bin/phios-capabilities` (S-04) via `Quickshell.Io.Process`, resolving the checkout the same way `phi`'s own `internal/tokens.Root()` does (`$PHI_DOTFILES`, else `~/phios-dotfiles`, S-12), falling back to a bare `PATH` lookup; runs once at `Component.onCompleted` and exposes a `refresh()` for later. `Config/Settings.qml` bridges onto `phi state get/set/list` (S-13, already real and installed on all three hosts) the same way, via a dynamically created `Process` per call; nothing calls it yet, the first consumer is the settings panel (S-40). **Interpretive call, surfaced to the user before proceeding, not decided unilaterally:** `phi-shell/CLAUDE.md` said "`Config/Appearance.qml` and `Config/Capabilities.qml` are the **only** files that touch framework types" — read strictly, that would forbid `Settings.qml`'s `Process` bridge, and even `Tokens.qml`'s required `Singleton` base class. Asked the user, who asked to check the roadmap first: S-32 (clipboard, `wl-paste --watch` via `Quickshell.Io`), S-42 (hyprsunset "driven from the shell via `Quickshell.Io` — a timer and a control call") and S-43 (spotlight overlay via the Hyprland event socket) all already plan `Quickshell.Io`/service usage in files that are neither `Appearance` nor `Capabilities` — the literal reading could not have held across the project as already planned. Read instead as scoped to the volatile *service* surface master plan §8.1 actually flags unstable at 0.3.x (`Quickshell.Services.*`, `.Hyprland`, `.Wayland`, `.Bluetooth`, `.Networking`), not the `Singleton` base class or `Quickshell.Io` primitives, which are foundational plumbing every config file needs. `phi-shell/CLAUDE.md` amended in the same commit to say this precisely, so it does not get re-litigated at S-32/S-42. Every QML construct used (`Singleton`, `ShellRoot`, `Variants` and its `delegate` default-property/`modelData` injection, `ShellScreen`, `Quickshell.screens`, `Process`/`StdioCollector` and their exact property and signal names) was checked against the real Quickshell source at `git.outfoxxed.me/quickshell/quickshell` rather than assumed, including one QML-language check that changed the code before it was ever written: QML property declarations (not functions) must start with a lower-case letter, so the "private" helper properties originally named `_cap`/`_out`/`_bridge` were renamed `capRaw`/`output`/`bridgeComponent` before committing. **Convention, not invented here:** phi-shell must be checked out at exactly `~/.config/quickshell/phi` — taken from `phios-user-runbook.md` §6.2's pre-existing `qs -p ~/.config/quickshell/phi` line, which predates this step; documented in `phi-shell/README.md` and is the destination the new `phios-dotfiles` adapter row assumes. On the `phios-dotfiles` side: a new template, `profiles/desktop/templates/.config/quickshell/phi/Config/Tokens.qml.tmpl` (profile `desktop`, matching kitty/mpv — phi-shell is `zotac`/`razer` only, master plan §8.4 says `mini` has no bar), and a new `design/adapters.txt` row, class A with reload `-` rather than `[unknown]`: Quickshell watches every QML file it has loaded and hot-reloads on change by itself (master plan §8.1, "hot reload al salvataggio", confirmed against the real `QuickshellGlobal.watchFiles` property in source) — there is no separate reload command to run or to fill in later, unlike every other `[unknown]` row. Confirmed off-machine with the real `phi` binary, not simulated: `phi theme list` shows the new row; `phi theme set dark --dry-run` proposes exactly one `create` for it under `[A]`, absent from the `unwired` section (unlike the `[unknown]` rows); a real `phi theme set dark` against a throwaway `$HOME` renders it correctly and a second run reports `ok` (idempotent). **No `phi`/`phi-packages` change was needed for this step**: `design/adapters.txt` is read at runtime from the `phios-dotfiles` checkout, not compiled into the `phi` binary, so this row takes effect on `zotac`/`razer` as soon as they `git pull` — no new tag, build or `pacman` install required. **Nothing here has ever been loaded by a real Quickshell process** — `qs -p` has not run anywhere, matching this milestone's own header warning ("You cannot run it. Every visual result is verified by the user"). `DONE WHEN` ("the shell process starts, produces no surface yet, and logs no errors") is therefore entirely the user's to confirm. **Second commit (`156e362`), before handoff, not after a failed verification:** an advisor pass over the first commit caught two defects that would very likely have made `qs -p` fail on first real use. First, `shell.qml`'s `import "./Config" as Config"` was a generic relative-path QML import; Quickshell 0.2+ has its own config-relative module syntax, `import qs.<path> [as <Namespace>]` (`qs` always resolves to shell.qml's own folder), which Quickshell's own guide recommends over a relative import as more LSP-friendly — and source evidence (`src/core/qsintercept.cpp`'s `qs:` URL scheme, which the interceptor explicitly preserves so that "objects created from those paths will [be] able to use singletons") indicates cross-directory `pragma Singleton` resolution goes through that mechanism specifically, not a generic directory import, which the fetched docs never actually confirmed for the cross-directory case despite extensive searching. Changed to `import qs.Config as Config`. Second, `shell.qml`'s startup log only touched `Config.Appearance` — QML singletons instantiate lazily on first use, so `Config.Capabilities` would never have been constructed, its `Component.onCompleted: refresh()` would never have fired, and the `Process`/`StdioCollector` probe inside it — verified against source but never actually exercised — would not have been real signal despite the file's own comment claiming otherwise. Fixed by also reading `Config.Capabilities.gpuVendor` in the same log line, so the first real run is an actual smoke test of that path, not just a parse check. Neither fix touches the `phios-dotfiles` side (adapters.txt/template are unaffected; already re-confirmed unchanged). **Flagged, not fixed, for whichever step first wires the session (S-24):** `quickshell` is approved in master plan §15 (M2, `extra`) but is not declared in any profile's `packages.txt` yet — the same undeclared-manual-install shape S-14 flagged for `smartmontools` on `mini`. The USER block below still has the user install it by hand, matching the S-20 AGENT bullets as written; declaring it is out of this step's stated scope. **First real verification round on `razer`, third commit (`phi-shell` `2b13da5`):** the user ran `qs -p` twice. Once over SSH, which failed with `qt.qpa.xcb: could not connect to display` — expected and not a bug, an SSH session has no Wayland compositor attached; this needs a terminal inside the graphical session itself, flagged for the next handoff. Once on `razer`'s own session, which found a real, load-blocking bug this agent had not anticipated despite the earlier source-level verification: `Config/Tokens.example.qml`, checked in beside the real generated `Config/Tokens.qml`, crashed the whole shell at load with `Config.Tokens.example - Tokens is not a type`. Cause: Quickshell's config-tree module scanner reads every `.qml` file inside a directory that gets `import qs.`'d, whether or not anything actually references that file, and a dot in the middle of a QML type filename is not a valid identifier — the scanner took `Tokens` (everything before the first dot) as the type name for **both** files and collided with the real one. This is exactly the gap the earlier per-type source verification could not have caught: it confirmed every type `phi-shell` code actually references is real and correctly named, but said nothing about Quickshell's own directory-scanning behavior toward a file nothing references at all. Fixed by moving the worked example to `phi-shell/docs/tokens-example.md` — a fenced code block in markdown, not a `.qml` file, so it cannot be swept into a module scan regardless of which directory it sits in; `phi-shell/README.md`, `shell.qml`'s comment, and this repository's own template header comment (`Tokens.qml.tmpl`) all updated to point at the new path. Re-verified off-machine that the template still renders cleanly (80 properties, header comment only affected). **Second real bug found on the very next `qs -p` run on `razer`, fourth `phi-shell` commit (`c911744`):** the `Tokens.example.qml` fix cleared that failure and the load proceeded further — `Config/Tokens.qml`, `Config/Appearance.qml` and `Config/Capabilities.qml` all loaded correctly (confirmed by the error trace reaching past them with no complaint) — and then failed inside `Config/Settings.qml` itself: `Component is not a type`. Cause: `Settings.qml` declares `property Component bridgeComponent: Component { Process { ... } }`, but `Component` is a `QtQml` type and the file only imported `Quickshell`/`Quickshell.Io` — a plain missing import, not a Quickshell-specific surprise this time. Fixed by adding `import QtQml`. The error trace itself confirmed something useful beyond the bug: `import qs.Config` validates every file in the directory as one batch at load time, not only the types a binding actually touches at runtime — the cascading "X unavailable" chain through `Appearance`/`Capabilities`, files that have no reference to `Settings` at all, is that batch validation surfacing through files it happens to process along the way, the same mechanism that made the unrelated, unreferenced `Tokens.example.qml` fatal in the previous round. Re-audited the other three `Config/` files' imports against every type they actually use (built-in primitives — `color`/`real`/`int`/`string`/`var` — and the always-available `Qt` global and `Component.onCompleted` attached signal need no import; only a bare `Component` **type** reference does) and found no further gap. **Correction to this row's own previous paragraph, caught on advisor review before a third failed round trip:** the claim that the missing seventh line was "very likely incomplete scrollback" does not hold — the load aborted inside `Capabilities.qml` on that run, so `Appearance.qml`'s bindings, including the ones reading `Tokens.bg0`, never actually evaluated; nothing in that transcript is evidence `Config/Tokens.qml` exists on disk on `razer` at all, and the six `ok` lines shown are exactly the six pre-S-20 adapters. This is now an open, unconfirmed question the next `USER` block checks directly (`ls -l`, `phi theme list`) rather than argued from indirect evidence again. **Fifth `phi-shell` commit (`0fdebe6`), same review, before sending the next round back:** rather than wait for the next single-error report (the loader aborts at the first fatal error, so every real run so far has surfaced exactly one missing import and no more), audited every property in all four `Config/` files against Qt's actual value-type documentation and found two more of the same class of bug pre-emptively: `Capabilities.qml` uses `Component.onCompleted` (needs `QtQml`, same failure shape as the `Settings.qml` fix — "Non-existent attached object") and `Appearance.qml` declares roughly twenty `property color` fields and calls `Qt.rgba()`, neither of which had been reached by the loader on any run yet — `color`/`font` and the `Qt.rgba`/`hsla`/`darker`/`lighter`/`tint` helpers specifically require `QtQuick`, not `QtQml` (confirmed against `doc.qt.io`, not assumed: `int`/`real`/`bool`/`string`/`var`/`list`/`date`/`double`/`url`/`variant`/`void` are QML-native and need no import at all, which is the actual, verified line between what needed fixing here and what didn't). **Standing lesson for whichever step next touches `phi-shell` QML, not just this one:** this loop — one real run, one missing import found, one fix, repeat — is structurally slow because a QML loader stops at the first fatal error. `qmllint` (part of `qt6-declarative`, a Quickshell dependency already installed wherever Quickshell is) reports every file's own type/import errors in one pass and runs headless, so it belongs in the `USER` block *before* `qs -p` on every future round that touches this repository's QML, not only used reactively after a crash. **`qmllint` itself turned out not to be on `razer`'s `PATH`** (`command not found`) — not chased further, since the next real `qs -p` run produced better signal anyway: `INFO: Configuration Loaded` — the shell genuinely starts now, past every import bug above, with only non-fatal `WARN`-level `ReferenceError: Tokens is not defined` messages (one per property in `Appearance.qml`) rather than a load abort. **Two more real findings, both root-caused with actual evidence rather than guessed at, sixth `phi-shell` commit (`c525360`):**
  1. `Config/Tokens.qml` still does not exist on `razer` (`ls -l` confirms) even after a fresh `phi theme set dark` — but this is not a bug in this step's work: `razer`'s `phios-dotfiles` checkout has two remotes, `github` (this session's own, matching exactly) and `origin` (`flavio@mini:/srv/git/phios-dotfiles.git`, the user's own server), and its `master` branch tracks `origin` — so a bare `git pull` there follows the user's own sync cadence from `mini`, not this session's pushes to `github` directly. Exactly the architecture `CLAUDE.md` already describes ("the user's server receives pushes only from the user"); nothing to fix here, the row will reach `razer` once the user's own `github`→`mini` sync happens (or by explicitly pulling `github` directly for testing).
  2. `gpu=none` in every run so far was never a probe failure — the user proved both `bin/phios-capabilities` directly and the *exact* `sh -c` command `Process` runs work correctly by hand, full output, `exit=0`, with `$HOME` set as expected. The real bug was a race baked into the verification itself: spawning `probe` and reading its stdout is asynchronous, so `shell.qml`'s log line, which reads `Config.Capabilities.gpuVendor` in the very same `Component.onCompleted` tick that calls `refresh()`, was always going to observe the still-unpopulated default — regardless of whether the probe actually succeeded underneath. Fixed by adding `onCapRawChanged: console.log(...)` inside `Capabilities.qml` itself, so the real value is logged reactively once it actually arrives, making the check meaningful instead of a coin flip decided by timing. **Lesson for future async-probe singletons in this shell:** a synchronous read of an async singleton's property, taken at `Component.onCompleted` time, proves the file *parses*, not that the async path inside it *works* — those need their own reactive confirmation, not a bare log line at startup. **This did not fully close the question, seventh `phi-shell` commit (`f076a94`):** the next real run, `~/.config/quickshell/phi` and `~/phios-dotfiles` both pulled fresh (the latter explicitly from `github`, bypassing `origin`'s slower sync per the note above), `phi theme set dark` now genuinely created `Config/Tokens.qml` (`create`, not the missing row from before), and the shell loaded with **`variant=dark` and zero `ReferenceError`s** — the `Tokens.example.qml`/`Settings.qml`/`Capabilities.qml`/`Appearance.qml` import chain is confirmed working end to end for the first time. But the reactive `onCapRawChanged` log fired twice, both times still `gpu=none` — including the second firing, which lands *after* `INFO: Configuration Loaded`, so it cannot be explained by the same startup race the previous commit fixed; something about the real, later-arriving probe result is still empty or unparsed. Since the user already proved the exact `sh -c` string this file runs produces correct output by hand (`exit=0`, `PHI_CAP_GPU_VENDOR=intel`), the remaining suspect is an environment difference between an interactive shell and Quickshell's own child process — most likely `$HOME` not reaching it. Rather than guess again, added a debug fallback (only reached if both the dotfiles-relative and bare-PATH invocations fail) that echoes `$HOME`/`$PATH`/the resolved dotfiles path, plus an unconditional raw-output log in the `StdioCollector` handler so the next run shows what `Process` actually received, not just the parsed (and possibly silently-empty-parsed) result. The new shell syntax was verified directly with real `sh -c` off-machine before committing — both the success path (a throwaway fake script standing in for `phios-capabilities`) and the failure/debug path — rather than trusted from the QML string alone, since three separate quoting/import mistakes earlier in this same step are reason enough not to trust a fourth one unverified. These debug lines are temporary and should come back out once the real cause is found. **The debug round settled it decisively, eighth `phi-shell` commit (`70e8883`), same round:** the raw-output log showed Quickshell's `Process` received exactly the correct, complete output (`PHI_CAP_GPU_VENDOR=intel` included, `razer` being a Razer Blade 13 2020 with no discrete GPU, confirmed by the user) — the same output the user's manual reproduction produced. Independently confirmed the parsing logic itself was never the bug either: ran the exact `onStreamFinished` parsing code, unmodified, in a throwaway Node.js script against the exact bytes the real run captured, and it produced the correct `gpuVendor: "intel"`. So neither the shell command, the environment reaching Quickshell's child process, nor the parser were ever at fault — all three hypotheses this row worked through in turn were each individually correct on their own terms and each still left the real bug standing. **Root cause:** `onCapRawChanged`'s `console.log` read the *derived* `Capabilities.gpuVendor` property (`capRaw.gpuVendor`) from inside a handler triggered by `capRaw`'s own change signal — and that specific indirection printed the stale default even on the exact call where `capRaw` had just been set correctly, a QML property-binding-propagation-ordering detail this step did not chase to ground (out of scope for a skeleton step; noted here in case a future singleton's reactive handler shows the same symptom). Fixed by logging `next.gpuVendor` directly at the point it is computed, with no property-binding indirection between the correct value and the log line. Also reverted the previous commit's debug-fallback echoes and raw-output dump entirely, since the hypothesis they were built to test is now disproven and they no longer serve a purpose — `Capabilities.qml`'s `Process` command is back to its original, simpler two-legged form. **Four real, distinct bugs found and fixed across this step's real-hardware verification, each isolated with actual evidence rather than guessed at in sequence:** a QML-invalid dotted filename colliding with a real type, two missing `QtQml`/`QtQuick` imports, and this property-indirection logging bug — none of which the extensive pre-commit source-level verification against real Quickshell/Qt documentation could have caught, since all four are either runtime behaviors (the last three) or a directory-scanning behavior distinct from any single file's own correctness (the first). **`DONE WHEN` confirmed on real hardware, both desktop hosts:** `razer` — clean load, `variant=dark`, zero `ReferenceError`s, `phi-shell: capabilities refreshed, gpu=intel` (a Razer Blade 13 2020, Intel-only, per the user — correct). `zotac`, tested by the user on their own initiative beyond what this step asked for — same clean load, `gpu=nvidia` — correct for the one host in this project with a discrete GPU, and good independent confirmation that `Config/Capabilities.qml`'s probe is genuinely reading real per-host hardware rather than coincidentally right on one machine. `mini` was not tested, consistent with master plan §8.4 ("`mini`: Nessuna barra") — it has no shell to run. **Not yet touched by this step, deliberately, and worth a note for whoever starts S-21:** the `quickshell` package gap flagged earlier in this row for S-24 is still open; `Widgets/`, `Services/`, `Bar/`, `Panels/`, `Launcher/` and the rest of master plan §8.2's tree do not exist yet and land with the steps that own them. **Correction, found during S-36 (2026-09-08), phi-shell commit `ee197b6`, the most severe instance of a bug found across the whole repo that session:** `Config/Capabilities.qml`'s hardware probe left `running` true after each run instead of resetting it in `onExited`. Because `Process.onFinished()` (`io/process.cpp`) calls `startProcessIfReady()` unconditionally as its last step, this meant `bin/phios-capabilities` has been respawning in a tight, uninterrupted loop from the moment `phi-shell` starts, on every real session run to date since this step — not a periodic re-probe, a continuous one. Fixed by setting `running = false` in `onExited`. Real-hardware CPU/battery impact from this specific defect was never measured before the fix (no `phi doctor`/`top` capture from that period isolates it), so its practical severity on `razer`/`zotac` is unconfirmed, only its mechanism. |
| S-21 | Styled widget library | verified | phi-shell 9ce095d..7e00a45 (2 commits) | 2026-09-08 | User confirmed the widget→state→rule table and DONE WHEN by reading the code and the handoff report; row moved to `verified` in S-22's commit per this file's own rule (a commit cannot record its own hash in advance). **Not yet reachable by any real `qs -p` run, and it will take more than a `git pull` to get there — flagged here so the first S-22 round doesn't misread the cause of a failure:** (1) `Config/Tokens.qml` on `zotac`/`razer` is `phi theme set`'s pre-S-21 render and has no `borderWidth` property yet; `Appearance.qml`'s new `_px(Tokens.borderWidth)` binding would read `undefined` and every border width — `Separator`'s entire thickness included — would be `NaN` until `phi theme set dark` (or `light`) is re-run against a `phios-dotfiles` checkout that actually has this step's `PHI_BORDER_WIDTH` token. (2) S-20's own row already recorded that `razer`'s `phios-dotfiles` checkout tracks `origin` (the user's `mini` server), not `github` directly — a bare `git pull` there follows the user's own sync cadence and will not pick up this session's push on its own. (3) Nothing imports `qs.Widgets` anywhere yet (`shell.qml` still only imports `qs.Config`), so even a fully-updated, fully-pulled `qs -p` gives zero signal about whether these eleven files parse — a clean load next to S-22's own new code is not evidence this step's files are sound, only that they were never reached. The mechanical `grep` below and a `qmllint` pass (suggested in the handoff, not run here — S-20's row already found it missing from `razer`'s `PATH`; it ships with `qt6-declarative`, typically at `/usr/lib/qt6/bin/qmllint`) are the only checks available before S-22 actually imports this directory. **Second commit (`7e00a45`), before handoff, not after a failed round-trip, same practice S-20 established:** an advisor pass caught `Segment.qml`'s `StyledText { id: text; text: root.label }` — an id identical to the object's own `text` property, a known QML ambiguity — renamed to `iconGlyph`/`labelText` to match `ListRow`'s existing naming; grepped every other file's `id:` declarations against its own property names afterward and found no second instance. Scope is exactly the S-21 AGENT bullets: ten types under `phi-shell/Widgets/` (`StyledText`, `StyledButton`, `StyledIcon`, `Pill`, `Segment`, `Popover`, `Panel`, `ListRow`, `Separator`, `Scrim`), no `Services/`/`Bar/`/`Panels/` — those land with the steps that own them (S-22 onward). **The referenced style-plan document does not exist in this checkout**, flagged here rather than asked about: `CLAUDE.md`, the agent brief and master-plan §6 all cite `phios-piano-stilistico-finale.md` (`docs/phios-piano-stilistico-finale.md` — not present; confirmed by listing every `.md` file in all four repositories), but every rule this step actually needs is quoted verbatim in the S-21 AGENT bullets themselves and independently restated in master-plan §8.5/§8.6 — two independent sources agreeing, not a conflict between two planning documents, so this did not block the step per `CLAUDE.md` rule 4's own "disagreement between two planning documents" test. Left open for whoever needs a *different* section of that document later. **Seven-state architecture** (style plan §12/master-plan §8.6: "stati trasversali obbligatori per ogni componente: default hover active/pressed focus disabled loading invalid"), applied honestly rather than uniformly faked: self-detected via `HoverHandler`/`TapHandler`/`activeFocus` on the four interactive controls (`StyledButton`, `Pill`, `Segment`, `ListRow`); parent-driven (plain settable booleans, no hit-testing of their own) on the six passive/decorative ones (`StyledText`, `StyledIcon`, `Separator`, `Scrim`, `Panel`, `Popover`) — each of those documents in its own file comment exactly which of the seven has a defined visual effect and which does not (`Separator`/`Scrim` are the honest extremes: only opacity/presence differ, hover/press/active/focus are structurally present but produce no visible change, stated plainly rather than faked with an unused `HoverHandler`). `Panel`/`Popover` are the one case where all seven are fully meaningful (a real bg/border/opacity surface), `StyledText`/`StyledIcon` fall in between (invalid/disabled/loading are real, hover/press/active/focus are not, since a label has no defined "pressed" look of its own). All ten route their precedence and colour recipe through one shared file, `Widgets/WidgetStates.js` (`resolve` — the seven-state precedence, disabled > invalid > loading > active/pressed > focus > hover > default; `surfaceColors` — the affordance recipe below; `opacityFor`; `contentColor`; `fontPixelSize`; `chToPixels`), which is what "zero duplication inside surface class 1" (master-plan §8.2's own words for this directory) actually means: one precedence and one colour table, not ten. **Affordance rules (style plan §6/master-plan §8.6), enforced in the widgets, one rule per bullet the AGENT card names:** "label di sistema -> basso contrasto, monocromo" — `StyledText`/`StyledIcon`'s `kind: "label"` renders `textMuted`, never coloured, and is the default reading for chrome text. "valore/dato -> colore Tier 2 solo su soglia" — `tone` ("error"/"warn"/"success"/"info") is opt-in and empty by default on every widget that has it (`StyledText`, `StyledIcon`, `Segment`); the widget never picks a threshold itself, it only renders the tone the caller already decided. "interattivo inattivo -> stesso peso, opacità ridotta" — `WidgetStates.opacityFor` fades disabled/loading by one shared constant (`INACTIVE_OPACITY = 0.45`, un-derived from any token since design/tokens has no opacity tier — flagged the same way other steps flag a placeholder ratio) without changing colour or weight. "interattivo attivo/selezionato -> inversione piena" — `WidgetStates.surfaceColors`'s `"active"` case swaps bg/fg to `accent`/`accentText` for every interactive control; `Pill`'s `checked` state is exactly this rule, which is also §8.6's own worked example ("componente toggle standard: pillola radius-pill, segmento attivo pieno in accento"). "glifo '>' -> riservato al solo punto di input attivo" — enforced structurally, not just by convention: `StyledIcon.qml`'s own file comment states outright that it deliberately has no `activeMarker`-style property, so no other widget in the directory *can* render this glyph; `ListRow` is the one place it appears, rendered directly in its own template, shown exactly when its resolved state is `"focus"` (keyboard navigation position — the "active input point"), never for hover/press/persisted-`active`. **Two real bugs caught before this commit, not after a failed round-trip** (this repository's own `CLAUDE.md`/`phi-shell/CLAUDE.md` both say nothing here can be run — every one of these had to be caught by re-reading rather than observing a failure): (1) `Appearance.qml`'s own documented contract — `space-N` tokens are stored in `ch` of `font-mono`, not px ("a caller that needs px measures the font itself and multiplies") — was violated in an early draft that used `Config.Appearance.space3` directly as a pixel margin; fixed by giving every widget that needs real pixel spacing (`Panel`, `StyledButton`, `Pill`, `Segment`, `ListRow`) its own three-line `TextMetrics` measuring the "0" glyph (the same definition CSS's `ch` unit uses) at the base chrome size, plus a shared `WidgetStates.chToPixels(chCount, chWidth)` for the arithmetic. (2) A duplicate `Behavior on <property>` declared both inside a reused widget's own file (`StyledText`'s internal `Behavior on color`) and again at its usage site (`StyledButton`'s label instance) or, identically, inside a base type (`Panel`'s `Behavior on opacity`) and again in a file extending it (`Popover`) — untested and unnecessary, since a `Behavior` attaches to the property itself and keeps animating any override regardless of which layer supplies the value; both redundant declarations were removed rather than left as an unverified risk. **One QML-mechanics question resolved by reading real Quickshell source rather than assumed, matching S-20's own established practice:** whether the shared `WidgetStates` helper could be a `pragma Singleton` QML type (colocated with the widgets the same way `Config/`'s four singletons resolve each other bare) — fetched `git.outfoxxed.me/quickshell/quickshell`'s `src/core/singleton.hpp` directly and found `Singleton: public ReloadPropagator` with no `DefaultProperty`, meaning it cannot host a nested child object (a `TextMetrics`) at all; reverted to a plain `.pragma library` `.js` file instead, which every consuming widget imports explicitly (`import "WidgetStates.js" as WidgetStates` — unlike a sibling singleton, a `.js` library is not resolved bare) since directory-implicit QML modules only ever register `*.qml` files as types, confirmed against Qt's own documented module-resolution rules rather than assumed. **Second QML-mechanics check, same practice:** `Segment`'s icon+label pair was drafted with a `Row` and `anchors.verticalCenter` on each child; verified against Qt's own `Row` documentation, which specifically forbids horizontal-axis anchors (`left`/`right`/`horizontalCenter`/`fill`/`centerIn`) on a Row's children, not the cross-axis one originally used — the code was still rewritten as a plain anchored `Item` (safer and avoids the restriction question entirely) rather than kept on the strength of a technicality. **phios-dotfiles side:** `design/tokens.common.sh` gains `PHI_BORDER_WIDTH='1px'` (Shape section, next to `radius-base`/`radius-pill`) — no `§6.3` token covered stroke width before this step (`Separator`, every `Panel`/`Popover`/`StyledButton`/`Pill` border and the generic focus ring all needed one), marked `[PLACEHOLDER]` in the same header block as `radius-base` for the same reason (unverified on real fractional-HiDPI output); `design/preview.tmpl` and `phi-shell/docs/tokens-example.md` both updated so neither goes stale. No `phi`/`phi-packages` change needed — `internal/tokens.parseInto` is a generic `KEY=VALUE` line parser (confirmed by reading `phi/internal/tokens/tokens.go` rather than assumed), so a new token key needs no code change there, matching S-12's own note about the same file. **`DONE WHEN` ("no widget contains a literal colour, size, or duration") verified mechanically, not by inspection:** `grep -n '#[0-9a-fA-F]\{3,8\}\|[0-9]px\|[0-9]ms' Widgets/*.qml Widgets/*.js` returns nothing. The only bare numeric literals anywhere in the directory are the `"0"` glyph text used to measure `chWidth` (not a size), `0`/`1` as the two endpoints of an opacity fade (not a size, colour or duration — the same category as the already-justified `INACTIVE_OPACITY` constant), and `Separator`'s `implicitWidth`/`implicitHeight` `0` on its non-thickness axis (no intrinsic length opinion, not a size decision — the consumer sizes it via anchors/layout). **Nothing here has been loaded by a real Quickshell process** — this step needs none, unlike S-20: `VERIFY` is "Agent reports the widget list and which style-plan rule each enforces," not a `qs -p` run, so there is no `USER`/machine round-trip to hand off. Fixed (`42c6bf3`). Before sending this back, re-verified the same question for every other external type this step introduced, this time checking the macro line itself rather than the class name alone: `UPowerDevice`, `UPowerDeviceState`, `BluetoothAdapter`, `NetworkDevice` and `DeviceType` all register under `QML_ELEMENT` with their own class name unchanged — the `Iface`-suffix indirection is specific to Pipewire's node wrapper in this Quickshell version, not a pattern repeated elsewhere in the four service modules this step uses. **Lesson for whichever step next introduces an external Quickshell type this shell has never used before:** confirming a property *name* against source is not the same check as confirming a property's *declared type* is really the QML-registered name — the two can diverge (as they did here) even when every property on the type is otherwise exactly as documented. **`DONE WHEN` confirmed by the user on both real hosts, second round:** `razer` — clean load, right-island order matches `modules.json` exactly (volume, network, battery, wifi, bluetooth, Φ, clock), volume's click-to-mute works, no `night mode` segment (correct, not built). Each `Timer`-triggered poll was, in practice, never actually paced by its own `Timer` at all: the first completion respawned the process immediately, in a tight loop, for as long as the module existed on screen (`Gpu.qml` only on `zotac`, capability-gated). | S-24 | Session integration | verified | phi-shell a8314bc · dotfiles 1dbc205..716104b (2 commits) | 2026-09-08 | **Nothing here has run on real hardware yet — every claim below is off-machine reasoning against the real Hyprland Lua API, not an observed result.** `PROGRESS.md`'s own rule applies to this row's own commit hash: it cannot record the `phios-dotfiles` hash that contains it, so only the `phi-shell` side is filled in above; `S-25`'s commit fills in the rest once known. **The Hyprland Lua API used below was checked against real upstream sources, the same discipline `phi-shell` steps established for Quickshell** (S-20 onward), not assumed from the existing file's four calls alone: `hl.on`/`hl.exec_cmd`/`hl.window_rule`/`hl.workspace_rule` and every field used were confirmed against `hyprwm/Hyprland`'s own real `example/hyprland.lua` (fetched raw, not summarized — the wiki itself sits behind a JS proof-of-work checkpoint this agent cannot solve, so field-level detail came from `hyprwm/hyprland-wiki`'s rendered pages instead) and cross-checked against a second source (`alejandrominaya.github.io/hyprland-lua-docs`) before writing anything. **`quickshell` package gap closed, flagged by name for this exact step in S-20's own row** ("Flagged, not fixed, for whichever step first wires the session (S-24): `quickshell` is approved in master plan §15 (M2, `extra`) but is not declared in any profile's `packages.txt` yet"): added to `profiles/desktop/packages.txt` (`zotac`+`razer`, matching `Config/Tokens.qml.tmpl`'s own profile placement since S-20; `mini` has no bar per §8.4). The three `qt6-*` dependency rows master plan §15 lists as "verificare quali siano già tirate dal pacchetto" were deliberately left undeclared, on the same reasoning this repository already applies elsewhere (`openrazer-daemon`'s row: dependencies a package's own `depends()` array pulls are not separately listed) — unverified against a real `pacman -Si quickshell`, since no machine is reachable from here. **Autostart** (`hyprland.lua`, `profiles/desktop`, a plain linked `home/` file, not a `templates/` one — confirmed by its own path, so no design token could be threaded through it even if one were needed): `hl.on("hyprland.start", function () hl.exec_cmd("pgrep -x qs >/dev/null || qs -p ~/.config/quickshell/phi") end)`, the exact shape the real upstream example itself documents for autostarting "notifications daemons, status bars, etc." (its own words, matching phi-shell's role exactly) — not invented syntax. Whether `hyprland.start` can refire on a plain `hyprctl reload` (as opposed to a fresh login) could not be confirmed from any source reachable here; the `pgrep` guard is written as cheap, always-safe insurance either way rather than a claim about confirmed behavior, and the real answer is explicitly left for `S-25` ("review the Hyprland Lua configuration against the version actually installed"). **Window rules — three-way split, not a single "window rules" blob:** (1) *Dynamic workspaces* (master plan §2.3, closed): implemented by omission — Hyprland's own default (create on use, destroy when empty) already is what the decision asks for, and the only way to violate it would be adding a static monitor-pinned or `default = true` rule, which this commit does not do; a comment records the reasoning so `S-25`'s compatibility review does not mistake the absence of a rule for the absence of a decision. (2) *btop workspace* (ADR 122 / `Q-N03`, closed at S-22, unbuilt since): `hl.workspace_rule({ workspace = "special:btop", persistent = true })` plus `hl.window_rule({ name = "btop-workspace", match = { class = "^phios-btop$" }, workspace = "special:btop" })`. The matched class is not Steam-style guesswork: it is a Wayland app id this agent itself assigns, in the same commit's sibling change to `phi-shell` (`Bar/modules/Gpu.qml`'s `btopLauncher.command` gains `--class phios-btop`, a real, standard kitty CLI flag confirmed against `man kitty` search results before use — a deliberate choice over matching btop's own window *title*, which a TUI can rewrite at runtime via its own OSC escape sequences and which this agent had no way to confirm one way or the other without a live run; matching a self-assigned class needed no such confirmation at all). This closes the mechanism S-23's own row named opinion this step would own verbatim ("the Hyprland window rule that would actually assign btop's window to that workspace is S-24's own AGENT bullet... unbuilt") — the direct `kitty -e btop` launch S-23 shipped as a stopgap still works exactly as before, now additionally landing on the right workspace once one exists; the still-unbuilt persistent bar toggle (flagged in S-22's own row, not owned by any step) means visiting that workspace still needs a manual `togglespecialworkspace btop`, not a click. (3) *Steam* (master plan §2.3: dedicated workspace, secondary windows floating) — **deliberately NOT written**, per the AGENT card's own explicit instruction, not this agent's judgment call: "Steam window rules need real class and title values. Ask the user for hyprctl clients output rather than guessing." This agent has no path to either machine to run that command (`CLAUDE.md` rule 4) and the card forecloses guessing outright — unlike every other placeholder value this project has shipped and flagged for cheap veto (`<mini-overlay-address>`, `[PLACEHOLDER]` tokens, provisional thresholds), a wrong Steam class is not a flaggable guess but the exact thing the card says not to do. A commented scaffold is left in `hyprland.lua` showing the three rules' shape with `<angle-bracket>` placeholders, mechanical to fill in once real data arrives — this is also why `VERIFY`'s existing "hyprctl clients for Steam windows" line is not decorative: it is where that data was always going to come from, one bullet the card and this row agree on without having to invent a second round-trip. **Hot-reload path documented** (AGENT: "so the user can iterate without restarting"), in `phi-shell/README.md` next to the pre-existing `qs -p` convention rather than in this repository, since the reload mechanism itself (Quickshell's own file-watcher, `S-20`) lives entirely in that repository's domain: QML edits need no restart at all (unchanged, pre-existing behavior, now just written down); a process-level restart, when genuinely needed, is `pkill -x qs && qs -p ~/.config/quickshell/phi`, with no Hyprland session reload involved either way. **Removed nothing**, per the AGENT card's own instruction — waybar was never installed, confirmed again by the unchanged `profiles/desktop/packages.txt` diff (only an addition). **`DONE WHEN` genuinely unverifiable from here in either half:** "starts automatically" needs a real login on `zotac`/`razer`, and "survives a shell reload" needs a real `hyprctl reload` (or Hyprland's own config-reload path) on real hardware — neither is something this agent can produce or has produced. The `USER`/`VERIFY` blocks below ask for exactly the two things this row is missing: a session restart on both machines, and `hyprctl clients` output while Steam is open (main window plus one secondary window, e.g. the friends list) so the deferred Steam rules can be written for real in a follow-up commit under this same step, before `DONE WHEN` can honestly be called met. **First real-hardware round, `razer` only, found and fixed a real bug before this row could be marked verified — not shipped and walked back later.** `DONE WHEN`'s own two literal criteria are met: `pgrep -x qs` showed `qs` running after the session restart (bar starts automatically, once Hyprland itself is up — see the session-startup gap below), and the user confirmed the session was unchanged after running `hyprctl reload` (survives a reload). But the `hyprctl clients` capture taken during the same round surfaced a real defect in the btop rule, not a clean pass: **every** window present — the user's own terminal running the capture command, and Steam itself — showed `workspace: -98 (special:btop)`, not just btop's own window. Root cause: the original rule assigned btop's window with a bare `workspace = "special:btop"`, no `silent` suffix, which (confirmed against real Hyprland source, not re-guessed) switches the monitor's active workspace to the target the moment the window opens, the same as a normal workspace assignment. With no workspace-switching keybinding built yet (`S-38`), there was no way back out once that happened, so everything opened afterward — the user's own diagnostic terminal, then Steam — landed on `special:btop` by simple inheritance of "whatever the active workspace happens to be." This also explains the user's second observation, that "the special:btop button does nothing when pressed": the GPU module's click only ever launches a fresh `kitty --class phios-btop -e btop` (S-23's stopgap, unchanged by this step, no toggle wired to any bar element per S-22's own flagged gap) — with the session already sitting inside `special:btop` from the earlier launch, a second click had nothing visibly different to switch to. **Fixed** (same commit as this row): `workspace = "special:btop silent"` — the window still lands on the special workspace, but nothing else follows it there, and the workspace stays hidden until something actually calls `togglespecialworkspace btop` (still unwired, left alone exactly as the user asked: "if that's planned, you should not change it"). **Not yet re-verified on real hardware** — this fix is reasoned from the same real Hyprland source the original (buggy) rule was, but has not itself been through a round-trip yet; the next `USER`/`VERIFY` block asks for exactly that, cheaply (reload, click the GPU module once, confirm nothing else moves workspace). **Steam's real class and title arrived in this same round**, closing the guessing question outright: `hyprctl clients` on `razer` showed Steam's actual main window as `class: steam, title: Steam, initialClass: steam` (xwayland: 1) — used verbatim, not adjusted. `hl.window_rule({ name = "steam-workspace", match = { class = "^steam$" }, workspace = "name:steam" })` added: a plain named workspace, not special and not `silent`, since — unlike btop — Steam is something you deliberately switch to, not a background peek. **"Secondary windows floating" is still not written**, and for the same reason the original card gave for not guessing Steam's class: the one real window captured is the main library window, and distinguishing a secondary one (Friends List, a chat window, a game's own popup) needs either that window's own real title, or a confirmed way to negate a match in this Hyprland version — real sources actively disagree on whether the regex engine here even supports a negative-lookahead title match (`ECMA-262`-style) or requires a separate `negative:` prefix (`RE2`-style), and guessing between two contradictory real sources is the same mistake as guessing a class name blind. Left as a commented scaffold; needs one more real `hyprctl clients` capture with a secondary Steam window open. **New, out-of-scope gap surfaced by the user, not fixed here, exactly per their own instruction** ("i think this is fine by the plans, no need to add things if those are covered in the plans later"): Hyprland itself did not start automatically on `razer` after the reboot — the user had to run `start-hyprland` by hand before `phi-shell` could autostart into it. This is a layer below "the shell starts with the session" (S-24's own goal, which is about `phi-shell` once Hyprland is already running): how Hyprland itself launches at login (`gestione sessione`, listed as "Da definire" in `phios-architettura.md` §8, line 638) has no owning step yet in any planning document read so far. Flagged here for whichever step or new card eventually closes it — not addressed, per the user's own explicit request. **Closed as `verified` on the user's own explicit instruction** ("if nothing is left to be done, you can mark it as verified"), with every remaining gap recorded here rather than silently dropped, none of them requiring a code change — they are coverage gaps in what could be tested today, not defects: (1) **Steam's dedicated-workspace rule is unconfirmed** — the user reported Steam still showing as "just a tiled window," not on a workspace of its own, and asked whether that was intended. Answer, for the record: yes and no. Tiled (never floating) is exactly right for the *main* window — master plan §2.3 only asks for secondary windows to float, and this rule was deliberately written without `float`. Landing on its own **workspace**, though, is a real Hyprland window-rule limitation, not a bug in the rule itself: a `workspace` assignment only fires when a window is first created, never retroactively, and the Steam window the user was looking at almost certainly predates this commit — it was already open from the previous round's `hyprctl clients` capture. Confirming the workspace assignment for real needs a full Steam quit and relaunch, not yet done; flagged here as a cheap, non-blocking check for whenever it's convenient, not requested as a follow-up round. (2) **The btop `silent` fix is unconfirmed on any host.** `zotac` — where `Bar/modules/Gpu.qml`'s GPU module actually appears, since it is gated on the nvidia-only `nvidiaGpu` capability (S-23) — was not reachable this round ("I cannot test the gpu on zotac currently"). `razer` has no GPU module in its bar at all (Intel-only, no `nvidia-smi`), so the original round's btop test there could only have exercised the window rule directly (by launching `kitty --class phios-btop -e btop` some other way, not a bar click) — asking the user to "click the GPU bar module" on `razer` in the previous handoff was this agent's own mistake, since no such element exists on that host's bar; recorded here so the next agent doesn't repeat it. The mechanism itself (`silent` suppressing the forced workspace switch) is reasoned from real Hyprland source, same as the original rule was, just not yet round-tripped anywhere. (3) **btop's special workspace has no way to be reached today, by design-so-far, not a defect**: no bar toggle exists (flagged since S-22, unassigned to any step) and no keybinding exists either (`S-38`, not built) — the user's own words, "without keybinds i can't reach it," confirm this is the expected, already-documented state, not a new gap. (4) **Hyprland's own session start is still manual** (`start-hyprland`, reported in the previous round) — reiterated here at the user's request to keep it on record; still no owning step in any planning document read so far, still deliberately not addressed. |

| S-30 | Notification daemon and toasts | awaiting-verification | phi-shell 559eb4d (bacc813 + correction) | 2026-09-08 | **Correction, same step, found in review before verification (§16.1: correct within the step, not a new one):** the first commit set `notification.tracked = true` on arrival and never set it back, so `server.trackedNotifications` (re-exported as `active`) accumulated every notification for the shell's whole lifetime — the sidebar's Active section would have grown without bound, every closed notification duplicated against its own History row with dead action buttons, and a DND-silenced notification (never shown as a toast, so nothing would ever have called `expire()` on it) would never have left `active` at all. Fixed in `559eb4d`: every tracked notification now gets a bounded lifetime via a per-notification one-shot `Timer` that calls the real `Notification.expire()` — respecting the sender's own `expireTimeout` if set, else 8s — whether or not DND ever let it become a toast; `tracked` is set back to `false` once the resulting `closed` signal fires and the history entry is recorded, which is also what correctly releases the object. `Notifications/Toast.qml`'s own now-redundant local dismiss timer was removed — the toast queue now advances through the same central `closed` handler instead of a second, disconnected timer. Assumed, not verified: calling `expire()` on an already-closed notification (e.g. the sender withdrew it first) is a safe no-op — consistent with how comparable D-Bus-style close-idempotent APIs behave elsewhere in this stack, flagged for cheap veto if real hardware says otherwise. Built entirely in `phi-shell` (no dotfiles/phi change needed — Quickshell's `NotificationServer` is built into Quickshell itself, no new package). `Services/Notifications.qml` claims `org.freedesktop.Notifications` directly (ADR 073), tracks live notifications (`server.trackedNotifications`, re-exported as `active`) and persists a capped (200), newest-first history to `$XDG_STATE_HOME/phi/notifications.json` via a new `Config/Paths.qml` singleton — the storage shape S-13's own row explicitly deferred to whichever step first needed it for a collection, not a `phi state` scalar. DND reuses the **real** key name, `toggle.dnd` (confirmed by reading `phi/internal/state/state.go`'s `Keys` map directly rather than guessing from the master plan's prose — the plan's own §5.6 table just says "dnd", the actual key is namespaced `toggle.dnd` alongside `toggle.night-mode`/`toggle.spotlight`/`toggle.chroma`; caught before commit, not after). `Notifications/Toast.qml` is one bottom-right layer-shell surface per screen (`shell.qml`'s `Variants`, same per-monitor pattern as `Bar.Bar`, ADR 077), showing exactly one toast at a time from a FIFO queue — app icon (via `Quickshell.iconPath`, text-only fallback if it fails to resolve, same conservative call S-23's Volume module made about unverified glyph coverage) plus a horizontally-scrolling excerpt, realizing the closed decision in master-plan §2.3/architettura §8.2.1 ("notifica come icona animata con testo scorrevole, dettaglio nel pannello") literally rather than as a stack of toast cards. Show/hide is category B (§6.5's own table names "notifiche e toast" under B); the marquee scroll itself is read as category A (continuous, linear-only) since it is not a state transition — a placement this agent judged, not one either closed-decision table names explicitly, flagged for cheap veto. **API verified against real Quickshell source before writing code** (git.outfoxxed.me → could not be fetched directly, 403; raw.githubusercontent.com/quickshell-mirror/quickshell mirror used instead, same repository): `NotificationServer`/`Notification`/`NotificationAction` full property and method list (services/notifications/{qml,notification}.hpp), `FileView.setText`/`loaded`/`loadFailed` (io/fileview.hpp — this is the first file in this repo to *write* via FileView, every prior use was read-only), `Quickshell.env()` (core/qmlglobal.hpp, confirmed synchronous, used instead of a Process-based resolution the way `Config/Capabilities.qml` needs). **Not verified, cannot be off-machine:** whether `NotificationServer` actually claims the bus name without a race against any other running notification daemon (none is declared in any profile, so none should be running, but this is unconfirmed on real hardware); whether the bottom-right anchor visually collides with anything; whether the marquee animation is smooth or janky at real frame rates; whether `Quickshell.iconPath` resolves real installed apps' icons correctly. **Held, not blocking:** verifying this step needs a way to send a manual test notification, and `notify-send` (package `libnotify`) is not declared in master plan §15 anywhere — flagged in the final question list rather than added unilaterally (CLAUDE.md rule 7/8); a `busctl` fallback that needs no new package is in the final VERIFY list. Also held: nothing in this milestone yet gives a human a way to *toggle* DND through the UI (that is S-40's settings panel, per §9.12) — VERIFY for this step drives `toggle.dnd` via `phi state set` directly and restarts `qs`, since nothing here polls the key live after startup. **Second correction, found during S-36 (2026-09-08), phi-shell commit `8a44c9f`:** the `$XDG_STATE_HOME/phi` `mkdir -p` safety `Process` (added alongside the first correction, `559eb4d`) was itself written with the same running-left-true respawn defect the S-13/S-20/S-23 corrections above also record — independent of, and in addition to, the `trackedNotifications` leak already fixed. Fixed the same way: `running = false` in `onExited`. **Third correction, found on real hardware (razer) during the M3 VERIFY round, phi-shell commit `c377099`:** `qs -p ~/.config/quickshell/phi` refused to load at all — `Services/Notifications.qml[58:12]: Duplicate signal name: invalid override of property change signal or superclass signal`, cascading into `Type X unavailable` up the entire import chain (`Bar.Bar`, every `Services/` bridge) since all of them transitively import this singleton. `property var history: []` already auto-generates a `historyChanged` signal; an explicit `signal historyChanged()` declared alongside it collided with that auto-generated one, which QML rejects outright rather than allowing silently. It was also never used — nothing anywhere in this repo connects to it — so `_pushHistory()`'s manual `root.historyChanged()` emit was redundant besides: the property assignment on the line above it already fires the auto-generated signal. Removed both. Swept every `.qml` file in `phi-shell` for the same pattern (an explicit `signal fooChanged()` alongside a `property ... foo`); this was the only instance. This is the same *class* of bug as the `Timer.restart()` name-shadowing catch in this same file's own header comment, just the signal-declaration form of it — and, unlike every other defect this session found by static reading or advisor review, this is the first one this milestone actually caught by running on real hardware, exactly as `phi-shell/CLAUDE.md`'s own "you cannot run this" line predicts should keep happening. **Fourth correction, same VERIFY round, phi-shell commit `fb79ed8`:** with the signal collision fixed, `qs -p ~/.config/quickshell/phi` got further and failed again — `Notifications/Toast.qml[77:17]: Cannot assign to non-existent property "opacity"`. Confirmed against the real Quickshell source, `src/window/windowinterface.hpp`: `WindowInterface` (`PanelWindow`'s base class) exposes `visible`/`color`/`width`/`height`/`screen`/`mask`/`data`/`contentItem` — no `opacity` anywhere. The `visible: opacity > 0` / `opacity: shown ? 1 : 0` / `Behavior on opacity` idiom this row's own header describes ("the same Behavior-on-opacity idiom every Widgets/ surface already uses") was written directly on the `PanelWindow` root, which does not have that property to animate — it silently compiled as an ad-hoc dynamic property doing nothing, so nothing caught it until QML actually tried to bind a `Behavior` against it. The same defect existed in five other files across four other steps (`S-31`'s `Sidebar.qml`, `S-33`'s `Launcher.qml`, `S-35`'s `Overview.qml`, `S-37`'s `AltTab.qml` and `Cheatsheet.qml`) — see each of those rows for a one-line cross-reference, full fix explanation kept here since `Toast.qml` is where the real error pointed first. Fixed in all six by moving the fade to a new inner `Item` (`fadeRoot`) wrapping the visual content — a real `Item` genuinely has `opacity`, and declaring `Behavior on opacity` there keeps it a bare local property rather than an untested dotted target like `Behavior on contentItem.opacity` (`contentItem` is real too, per the same header, but this repo has no local QML engine to confirm `Behavior on` accepts a dotted path, so the safer, unambiguous form was used instead). The window's own `visible` now reads `root.shown || fadeRoot.opacity > 0`, so it stays mapped until the fade-out animation actually finishes rather than vanishing mid-fade. Swept the rest of the repo for the same pattern: `Tooltip.qml`, `Scrim.qml`, `Popover.qml` also use `visible: opacity > 0`, but all three are `Item`-rooted, not `PanelWindow`-rooted, so their `opacity` is real and this bug does not apply to them. **Fifth correction, real-hardware VERIFY feedback (not a load error this time):** the `busctl` fallback command in this row's own VERIFY block failed (`busctl: unrecognized option '-1'`) — the user approved adding `libnotify` instead (dotfiles commit `3f405c6`, master plan §15.3 updated to match), so future verification uses `notify-send "S-30 verification" "Test notification"` in its place. |
