reopening that list was not this step's call to make, flagged for cheap veto if the user wants it reopened). **Two real bugs found and fixed before committing, not after:** (1) a first draft of the AI chat message bubble used QML's inline-component syntax (`component Bubble: Widgets.Panel {...}`) nested inside the tab's own root `Item` — confirmed against Qt's own QML-documents documentation that inline components cannot be nested and must be file-top-level, so it was pulled out into its own file, `Panels/tabs/ChatBubble.qml`, reached via the same same-directory `import "." as X` shape `Bar/Bar.qml` already uses for its `modules/` subdirectory, rather than leave an unverifiable language feature in the one file this agent could least afford to guess on. (2) Both the chat bubble and the notifications tab's active-notification card first wrote `parent.text`/`width: parent.width - parent.padding * 2` reaching for the *outer* `Widgets.Panel`'s own properties from a *nested* child — wrong, because `Widgets.Panel`'s default `content` property aliases into an inner `contentItem`, so a child's real runtime `parent` is that inner item, not the Panel itself; `.width` still resolves correctly by coincidence (`contentItem` has a real width too), but `.padding`/`.text` do not exist there at all. Fixed by giving each outer component an explicit `id` and referencing it directly (`bubbleRoot.text`, plain `root.chWidth` instead of a second padding subtraction) rather than relying on `parent`. **Not verified, cannot be off-machine:** whether the sidebar visually fits at 48ch on either machine's real display scale; whether the IpcHandler call actually reaches a running instance; whether the AI chat tab's static layout looks "finished" rather than obviously fake; whether `Quickshell.iconPath`-style app icons (not used here, but the same font/glyph caution applies to nothing in this tab) render correctly — this tab uses no icons or glyphs at all, deliberately, same conservative call S-23's Volume module made. **Correction, found on real hardware during the M3 VERIFY round, phi-shell commit `fb79ed8`:** `Sidebar.qml`'s root-level `Behavior on opacity` was written against a property `PanelWindow` does not have — see `S-30`'s row for the full explanation and fix, applied here identically (a new inner `Item`, `fadeRoot`, carries the real opacity and its `Behavior`). **Preventive fix, not a reported bug, phi-shell commit `56c6b4f`:** the sidebar itself toggles correctly on real hardware (confirmed by the user), but applied the same `Services/LayerFocus.qml` fix as `S-33`'s row anyway, since the AiChat tab's text input would hit the identical `WlrKeyboardFocus.None` default the moment it was actually typed into — not yet directly tested, but the root cause is confirmed, not guessed, and every other keyboard-driven surface in this milestone had it. |
| S-32 | Clipboard history | awaiting-verification | phi-shell 6c4f378 · dotfiles (this commit) | 2026-09-08 | `Services/Clipboard.qml` captures via one long-lived `wl-paste --watch sh -c '<script>'` process — same integration shape as `cliphist` itself (`wl-paste --watch cliphist store`), the tool this step removes (C-07, see below). No manifest file: the capture script is plain POSIX `sh` with no JSON writer available inside it, so the filesystem is the structure — `<id>.data`+`<id>.mime` per entry under `$XDG_STATE_HOME/phi/clipboard/entries/` (`id` = `date +%s%N`, so lexicographic sort is also chronological sort), plus a single `latest` file a watched `FileView` (`watchChanges: true`) reacts to so new arrivals show up without a polling timer. **Sensitive exclusion happens inside the capture script itself**, before anything ever reaches disk: `wl-paste --list-types` checked for `x-kde-passwordManagerHint`, dropped silently if present — cross-dependency on the M6 secrets decision (KeePassXC vs. Vaultwarden) already on record: if the eventual manager doesn't set this hint, the exclusion needs reimplementing against whatever it does instead. Pin state (`pins.json`) is the one piece of real JSON in this design, since pinning is a UI action, not a capture-time decision — Quickshell writes it directly. TTL sweep every six hours deletes unpinned entries past 30 days ("minimo un mese," exact value explicitly deferred to real consumption by the step card itself). `Panels/tabs/Clipboard.qml` replaces S-31's placeholder: inline Pin/Copy/Delete buttons per row — **not** the ContextMenu widget, honoring the user's own S-37-planning decision to build that widget without wiring it to any real surface yet. **Three real bugs found and fixed before committing:** (1) a preview-text property on a `FileView` was first named `text`, colliding with `FileView`'s own real `text()` function (confirmed via `io/fileview.hpp` — this file's own header now documents the fix, and flags it as the same shadowing class already caught twice in S-31's `Notifications.qml`/`ChatBubble.qml`) — renamed to a distinct binding, no property collision. (2) **The more serious one, and new to this step:** every one-shot `Process` (the directory listing, the per-entry mime reader, `restore`, `rm`) initially left `running: true` set after completion. Read directly from the real Quickshell source (`io/process.cpp`, not assumed): `Process::onFinished()` calls `startProcessIfReady()` as its *last* step, after the exited signal has already run — so a one-shot command with `running` never reset to `false` respawns itself the instant it exits, forever. Every one-shot `Process` in this file now sets `running = false` in its own `onExited` before (or as well as) destroying itself; only the long-lived `wl-paste --watch` process is deliberately left to auto-restart this way, which is a genuine, useful reading of the exact same mechanism (if the watcher itself ever dies unexpectedly, e.g. a compositor restart, it now comes back on its own with no code written for that case specifically). (3) The same `Widgets.Panel`-`contentItem`-indirection bug S-31 already found (a nested child's real `parent` is Panel's inner `contentItem`, not the Panel itself) was checked against every `parent.width`/`parent.padding` reference in this step's new files before committing, not found again — `.width` references are safe (`contentItem` genuinely has one), and this step's code never reaches for a Panel's own custom property from a nested child the way S-31's first draft did. **`cliphist` removed from `profiles/desktop/packages.txt` (C-07); `wl-clipboard` kept.** **Real, load-bearing gap found while doing this, not previously documented anywhere:** `bin/lib/packages.sh` has no removal logic at all — grepped directly, zero matches for `pacman -R`/`uninstall`/`remove` — the installer only ever adds what `packages.txt` newly declares, it never uninstalls a package that stops being declared. Removing the `cliphist` line here does **not**, by itself, uninstall `cliphist` from either machine, and `phi doctor`'s package-categories check (S-14) will not catch this drift either, since `cliphist` is a normal repo package, not foreign/AUR — the check that would flag it does not exist. A manual `sudo pacman -Rns cliphist` on both `zotac` and `razer` is now in the final VERIFY list for this reason, and this gap itself belongs on the question list: either the installer needs real package-removal logic (a bigger change, out of scope for this step to just add unilaterally), or every future "remove a package" step needs to keep naming the manual command by hand. **Not verified, cannot be off-machine:** whether `wl-paste --watch` actually behaves as documented under real Wayland/Hyprland (this whole design rests on the same integration contract `cliphist` itself uses, but this step could not run it); whether the sensitive-exclusion check actually catches a real KeePassXC copy (no password manager is installed by any profile yet — M6 — so this is unverifiable end-to-end until then); whether image capture via `wl-paste --type image/png` produces a file `imv`/other viewers can actually open. **Correction, found on real hardware during the M3 VERIFY round, phi-shell commit `0deb3ec`:** `Panels/tabs/Clipboard.qml` used `FileView` (its preview loader) without importing `Quickshell.Io`, the module that defines it — `qs -p ~/.config/quickshell/phi` failed with "FileView is not a type" once it reached this tab through `Sidebar.qml`. Every other file in this repo using `FileView` already imports it correctly; swept the rest of the repo for the same class of gap (a `Quickshell.Io` or other `Quickshell.*` submodule type used without its import) and found no second instance. Fixed with one added import line. **Second correction, real-hardware VERIFY feedback, phi-shell commit `56c6b4f`:** with the shell finally loading, copying text still never updated the sidebar's clipboard history. `Services.Clipboard` is a lazy `pragma Singleton`, same as every Quickshell singleton — unlike `Services.Notifications` (always instantiated early via `Notifications/Toast.qml`'s own top-level `Services.Notifications.activeToast` read, since `Toast.qml` is unconditionally instantiated per screen), nothing referenced `Services.Clipboard` until the sidebar's Clipboard tab was opened for the first time, so the `wl-paste --watch` capture process never started before that. Fixed by extending `shell.qml`'s own `Component.onCompleted` — already there specifically to force `Config` singletons to instantiate early, same mechanism, same reasoning — to also touch `Services.Clipboard.entries`. |
| S-33 | [BLOCKING] Launcher | awaiting-verification | phi 23a73c5 (tag v0.6.0) · phi-packages 96090ba · phi-shell 105eb0f | 2026-09-08 | **`phi` side (`internal/query`, ADR 018 — ranking and providers live here, the shell is a renderer only):** cold start is the constraint that shaped every design choice (`phi/CLAUDE.md`: "invoked on every keystroke... order of milliseconds") — `phi query <text>` is a fresh process per keystroke, not a persistent server, so every external-command provider runs **concurrently** (goroutines) bounded by a 120ms `providerTimeout`, and a slow provider degrades only itself, never the whole query. A native smoke-test run (this machine, not a phiOS host, so most providers short-circuited via `exec.LookPath`) measured **~14ms end to end** — promising, not conclusive; real timing with real `hyprctl`/`fd`/`zoxide` calls succeeding needs real hardware. Nine providers wired into `Providers()`, one per file: **calculator** (a small hand-written recursive-descent arithmetic parser — `+ - * / % ^` with parens and right-associative `^`, plus length/mass/temperature unit conversion, e.g. `10 km to mi`), **currency** (a tenth, added beyond the card's own list — `100 usd to eur`; the live API, `api.frankfurter.dev`, needs no key and was **verified with a real GET request from this environment before writing the file** — confirmed response shape, not assumed — but `providerTimeout` cannot fit a real HTTPS round trip, so `Query` only ever reads an on-disk cache and a stale/missing entry triggers a background refresh for the *next* query; the integration has never run end to end, flagged plainly as verified-API/unverified-integration), **applications** (`.desktop` scan, XDG dirs, its own INI-ish parser, tested against fixture content including a `[Desktop Action ...]` section that must NOT leak into the result — real gap this step's own tests caught), **windows** (`hyprctl clients -j`, parsed against a fixture shaped from S-24's own real captured JSON), **command** (Q-74's heuristic, implemented exactly as the card states it and flagged for the user's veto — single token is left to the applications provider entirely), **zoxide** (`--list`, preserving zoxide's own ranked order via a descending explicit score rather than re-deciding it), **ssh hosts** (`~/.ssh/config` `Host` lines, wildcards excluded), **web search** (DuckDuckGo — this agent's own pick, no document names an engine, flagged), **files** (via `fd`, already pre-approved in `packages.txt` — a **live** search, never a persisted index, so I-08's index-placement constraint does not apply), **system actions** (lock/suspend/log out only — ADR 021's own named non-verbs, handed to the shell as plain data). **Skipped, per this step's own scoping decision, not an oversight:** the password-vault provider (`Q-N09` — no password manager exists anywhere in the roadmap yet, M6; carried to the final question list) and dictionary/translation (the card's own words: "defer; they belong to a system translation backend which is not yet decided"). Ranking (`rank.go`) is a five-tier title-match heuristic (exact / prefix / word-prefix / substring / subsequence) plus a frecency term; frecency (`frecency.go`) is the storage shape `S-13`'s own `Keys` map named this step to define — a JSON file at `$XDG_STATE_HOME/phi/frecency.json`, deliberately outside `phi state`'s closed key set, one writer (`phi query record <id>`, called by the shell only on actual selection, never per keystroke). **Both are pure and carry real `go test` coverage — 30 test functions across the package, all passing, `go vet` and `gofmt -l` clean** — this is the off-machine substitute for the card's own instruction to "validate `phi query` in a terminal against `fzf` before any GUI work," which this agent cannot run; the twenty-queries-the-user-actually-types the card wants are guessed at in `rank_test.go`'s own comment, flagged for extension once real usage reports what actually ranked wrong. One real test bug caught before commit: `TestMatchWeightTiers`'s own "substring" case (`"kitty t"` against `"Kitty Terminal"`) was actually a **prefix** match by coincidence, not a substring one — the test's expectation was wrong, not `matchWeight`; fixed the test case, not the code. Tagged and pushed `v0.6.0`; `phi-packages` `pkgver` 0.5.0 → 0.6.0. **`phi-shell` side (`Launcher/Launcher.qml`):** every keystroke debounces 80ms then spawns `phi query` fresh, parses its JSON, renders it — no ranking logic here at all, matching ADR 018 on the shell side of the line. Opened via a `Quickshell.Io.IpcHandler` (`target: "launcher"`, same mechanism S-31's sidebar already uses), since S-38 still owns the real keybinding. Navigation stack: level 0 is the search field plus result list; Tab on a highlighted "command" result pushes the one concrete sub-view this step has real grounds to build (ADR 022) — an editable command line with a Run button; Escape pops one level or closes the launcher from the root. Action execution maps all eight `Action` kinds to a real effect via `Quickshell.execDetached` (no `Process` object needed for a fire-and-forget call): `exec`, `execTerminal` (`kitty --hold`, so a failing command's output survives instead of vanishing with the window — this step's own answer to the card's "define a hold policy," biased toward never losing output), `activateWindow` (`hyprctl dispatch focuswindow`), `openURL` (`xdg-open`), `copyText` (`wl-copy`), `changeDir`, `system` (lock via `loginctl lock-session` — the correct integration point for S-34's not-yet-built lock screen to listen on later, a harmless no-op today; suspend via `systemctl suspend`; logout by literally copying hyprland.lua's own Super+M binding, so the two paths to the same action can never disagree), and `pushView`. **Two real binding bugs found and fixed before committing, both the same class, both caught by re-deriving from first principles rather than assumed correct:** `text: root.queryText` (and the identical shape on the command sub-view's field) looked like live two-way sync but is not — assigning to a QML property that has an active binding **breaks that binding**, and `TextInput`'s own C++ side does exactly that assignment on every keystroke the user types, so after the very first character the field would silently stop tracking its source property at all; a later reset (closing the launcher, popping a view) would then never reach it. Fixed by making both fields one-way-out only (`onTextChanged` writes into the tracking property, never reads from it) and handling the one moment each field genuinely needs to change from the outside — opening, closing, pushing a view — with an explicit imperative assignment instead. Every one-shot `Process` here (the per-keystroke query, the selection-record call) resets `running = false` in its own `onExited` before destroying itself, the same `startProcessIfReady()` respawn trap S-32 found and documented directly from `io/process.cpp`. **Known, not fixed, flagged rather than silently shipped:** a query that arrives out of order (the user types past a slow response) can briefly show stale results — no sequence-number guard was added; this is real but judged non-critical polish, not a correctness bug, given cheap-veto budget was spent on the two binding bugs instead. **Not verified, cannot be off-machine:** the whole shell-side path end to end (spawning `phi`, parsing its output, rendering, executing an action) — nothing here has run inside a real Quickshell process; whether 80ms debounce feels responsive; whether `kitty --hold`/`hyprctl dispatch focuswindow`/`loginctl lock-session` behave as documented on real Hyprland. **DONE WHEN is explicitly not met and cannot be** ("the first result is right for the twenty queries the user actually types" needs a week of real typing, not a single session) — this row stays `awaiting-verification` on that basis alone even once every other check above passes. **Correction, found on real hardware during the M3 VERIFY round, phi-shell commit `fb79ed8`:** `Launcher.qml`'s root-level `Behavior on opacity` was written against a property `PanelWindow` does not have — see `S-30`'s row for the full explanation and fix, applied here identically. **Second correction, real-hardware VERIFY feedback, phi-shell commit `56c6b4f`:** with the shell loading, the launcher overlay appeared but the search field never received keyboard input at all — typing went to whatever window was underneath, clicking did nothing. Confirmed against the real source (`src/wayland/wlr_layershell/wlr_layershell.hpp`): `PanelWindow` defaults its attached `WlrLayershell.keyboardFocus` to `WlrKeyboardFocus.None` ("no keyboard input will be accepted") — correctly relied on by `Notifications/Toast.qml`, which must never steal focus, but never set anywhere that actually needs input. `searchField.forceActiveFocus()` only moves focus within the Qt Quick scene; it does nothing if the surface itself was never granted OS-level keyboard focus. Fixed via a new `Services/LayerFocus.qml` (setting it to `OnDemand`), kept in `Services/` per `phi-shell/CLAUDE.md`'s own rule confining `Quickshell.Wayland` there. |
| S-34 | Lock screen | awaiting-verification | phi-shell 812889a · dotfiles (this commit) | 2026-09-08 | **`Lock/Lock.qml` uses `WlSessionLock` on the real `ext-session-lock` protocol** (`Quickshell.Wayland`, verified against the real header, `wayland/session_lock.hpp`, not assumed), never a fullscreen window — the "stays locked if the client crashes" guarantee is the compositor's, confirmed in that header's own words: a destroyed `WlSessionLock` or a `quickshell` exit while locked leaves the screen "locked and painted with a solid color... inoperable," never exposed. **The security-critical part** (`S-34`'s own words: "the only genuinely security-critical code in the whole shell"): a single `PamContext` (`Quickshell.Services.Pam`, `config: "phi-shell-lock"`) declared **once**, deliberately outside the `surface` component `WlSessionLock` instantiates per screen — a `PamContext` placed inside that component would have meant one independent, concurrent PAM conversation per monitor, which is wrong. `PamResult` has exactly four values (`Success`/`Failed`/`Error`/`MaxTries`, confirmed against the real header, `services/pam/conversation.hpp`), and the `completed` handler has exactly one branch that unlocks (`Success`) and one default that does not — **ambiguous is not authenticated by construction**, not by discipline: there is no third branch to write wrong. Connected via `pam.completed.connect(...)` inside `Component.onCompleted`, not a direct `onCompleted:` handler on the `PamContext` itself — `PamContext`'s own signal is also named `completed`, and writing `onCompleted:` directly on that object risks colliding with the universal `Component.onCompleted` attached-property mechanism; `.connect()` on the real signal reference sidesteps the ambiguity entirely rather than relying on an untested precedence rule for the single most security-sensitive line in this codebase. **Locking is exposed to any same-user process via a `Quickshell.Io.IpcHandler`** (`qs ipc call lock lock`) — safe, since locking is never a security problem — **but unlocking has no IPC path anywhere.** The only place `locked` is ever set back to `false` is inside the `PamResult.Success` branch; nothing else in this file or reachable from outside it can clear the lock. **Real IPC-syntax correction made in this same commit, before it could ship wrong in three places at once:** the `qs ipc call <target> <function>` invocation had been written everywhere (S-31's `Panels/Sidebar.qml` comment, S-33's `Launcher.qml`) with an unconfirmed `-p ~/.config/quickshell/phi` flag guessed by analogy to `qs -p <path>` (which *launches* a shell at that config) — re-checked against the real Quickshell documentation's own worked example (`io/ipchandler.hpp`'s `qs ipc call rect setColor orange`) while building this step's own lock IPC call, which shows **no `-p` flag at all**, auto-targeting the one running instance; fixed in all three places in this same commit rather than leaving the discrepancy for whoever tested it first to discover. `Launcher.qml`'s "Lock" system action, which S-33 had wired to `loginctl lock-session` with a note that S-34 was "the correct integration point... to listen on later," now calls this `IpcHandler` directly instead — simpler, fully within this shell's own control, and does not depend on this project ever building a `systemd-logind` `Lock`-signal listener it does not need. `profiles/desktop/system/etc/pam.d/phi-shell-lock` (new `system/` directory for the `desktop` profile — did not exist before this step) is `/etc` material, **never applied by this repository**: `auth`/`account`/`password`/`session` all `include system-auth`, deliberately **not** `PamContext`'s own default service name (`"login"`), because that Arch service is configured for the text-console `login` program and may carry `pam_securetty`-style modules that assume a real controlling tty — a graphical, no-tty lock invoked against it could fail every attempt for a reason with nothing to do with the password being correct; this mirrors the same minimal service every other Wayland lock screen on Arch (`swaylock`, `hyprlock`) ships for itself. **A consequence worth naming plainly: until the user applies this file by hand, `PamContext.start()` cannot open a real PAM session at all** (`config: "phi-shell-lock"` names a file that does not exist on the machine yet) — this surfaces as `PamError.StartFailed`, itself just another non-`Success` outcome the existing fail-closed branch already handles correctly, so the screen stays locked with no path in either state, before or after the file is applied. Content beyond the password field: clock (`Qt.formatTime`/`formatDate`, a plain 1s `Timer`), battery (`Services.PowerBridge`, already built at `S-23`), now-playing media via a **new** thin `Services/Mpris.qml` wrapper (`Quickshell.Services.Mpris`, verified against the real header — `services/mpris/watcher.hpp` confirms the QML singleton name `Mpris` and `.players.values` as the real, JS-iterable form, `core/model.hpp`), and the three most recent notifications, read-only, from `Services.Notifications.history` (`S-30`). Reminders explicitly **not** built: no such feature exists anywhere else in this project, and the card itself lists them as "optionally." **Not verified, cannot be off-machine, and this is the step where that matters most:** whether `WlSessionLockSurface`'s `data` default property gives child items a real, properly-sized `parent` the way `PanelWindow` already does for every other surface in this repo — reasoned by analogy (both are Quickshell window-like types exposing real geometry, unlike `Widgets/Panel`'s specific `contentItem` alias workaround) but never exercised; if wrong, the visual content could be malpositioned or invisible while the lock itself stays fully secure regardless, since that guarantee is the protocol's, not this layout's — this is exactly what the VERIFY block's "lock, unlock" step will catch. Also unverified: the actual PAM conversation flow end to end (`responseRequired`/`responseVisible`/`respond()`) against a real `system-auth` stack; whether killing the shell process while locked truly leaves the screen inoperable as the protocol promises — this is the step's own explicit `USER` instruction and the one test that most matters here. **Correction (made post-hoc, during the M3 verification write-up, before any USER block shipped with the bug in it):** the "no `-p` flag at all" claim in this row's own IPC-syntax-correction paragraph above was itself wrong. It reasoned from the real `io/ipchandler.hpp` worked example, which is correct as far as it goes — but that example covers Quickshell's *default* config location (`<xdg dir>/quickshell/shell.qml`), never checked against how `hyprland.lua`'s own `AUTOSTART` section actually launches phi-shell: `qs -p ~/.config/quickshell/phi`, a *named* path, not the default. Confirmed by reading Quickshell's own `src/launch/parsecommand.cpp`: `ipc call` accepts an "Instance Selection" group (`-i/--id`, `--pid`) *or* a "Config Selection" group (`-p/--path`, `-c/--config`, mutually exclusive with instance selection) — with neither passed, it resolves the *default* config's instance, which is not phi-shell's. `-p ~/.config/quickshell/phi` was never wrong to include; removing it in this step was the actual mistake. Reverted in `Launcher.qml`, `Sidebar.qml`'s comment, and `AltTab.qml`'s comment (`phi-shell`, separate commit), and fixed at the only place it mattered on a real machine — every `qs ipc call` in `hyprland.lua`'s `S-38` keybindings and `S-35`'s gestures, all of which were shipped with the same bug (this commit, via a new shared `qsIpc()` Lua helper rather than six more hand-typed copies of the flag). **Third correction, real-hardware VERIFY feedback, phi-shell commit `e63f44e`:** `qs ipc call lock lock` returned "Target not found" — `qs ipc show` confirmed `lock` entirely absent while every other target (`sidebar`/`launcher`/`cheatsheet`/`overview`/`screenshot`/`record`/`alttab`) was present. Root cause confirmed against the real source, `src/wayland/session_lock.hpp`: unlike `PanelWindow` (used by every other surface in this repo, default property `data`, a list), `WlSessionLock`'s own default property (`surface`) is a **singular** `QQmlComponent*` — it can hold exactly one value. This file had four bare positional children (`PamContext`, a `Timer`, the `IpcHandler`, and `WlSessionLockSurface`) all implicitly competing for that one property; only the `IpcHandler` was confirmed missing, but whether `PamContext` or the `Timer` were silently dropped the same way is **not confirmed either way** — they occupied the identical position in the tree, so it is plausible the entire PAM flow was never actually running before this fix, not only the IPC trigger. Fixed by giving each an explicit, uniquely-named property instead of a bare child, and wrapping `WlSessionLockSurface` in an explicit `Component {}` for `surface:` rather than relying on implicit auto-wrap alongside other named properties. This introduced (and immediately fixed in the same commit) an `id: pam`/`id: retryTimer` collision against the same-named properties on `root` — the identical ambiguity class `S-21` already found once (`Segment.qml`'s `id: text`) — resolved by removing both ids and using `root.pam`/`root.retryTimer` explicitly everywhere, including inside `surface`'s `Component` body, which now crosses a component boundary that did not exist before this fix and has not been independently verified on real hardware. **Not verified, cannot be off-machine, and this is now more consequential than before:** whether `PamContext` was actually running prior to this fix (if not, no lock/unlock attempt on real hardware before this fix ever really exercised the security-critical PAM path at all); the full PAM conversation end to end after this fix — accepting input, a correct password unlocking, a wrong one not — and the kill-while-locked test, still this row's own single most important check. **Real incident, not a hypothetical — the user was stranded locked out of their own session testing this fix:** `PamContext.start()` failed immediately because `/etc/pam.d/phi-shell-lock` had never actually been installed (the manual `sudo install` step from an early handoff was missed). That fires PAM's `error` signal, never `completed` — and `retryTimer` was only ever restarted from `completed`, so nothing in the running session could recover on its own, and the password field's old `enabled: root.pam.responseRequired` binding stayed permanently false since PAM never got far enough to request a response. **`loginctl unlock-session` does not work against this `WlSessionLock` setup at all** — confirmed against every session `loginctl list-sessions` listed on `razer`, including the real seat0 session, all three tried, none had any effect. **Actual recovery needed a real Hyprland safety valve this project had no record of:** `pkill qs` followed by `hyprctl --instance 0 eval 'hl.clear_crashed_lockscreen()'` — worth recording plainly since it directly contradicts `WlSessionLock`'s own documented guarantee ("a dead client leaves the screen locked and inoperable, no way back in"); Hyprland provides an override the protocol documentation doesn't mention. Saved to this agent's own persistent memory so it isn't relearned the hard way twice. **Fixed in the same phi-shell commit (`d88bb01`) as the IPC-registration fix's own follow-up, after a second advisor review specifically because of the stakes:** `error.connect` now retries too, but through a separate, deliberately slower timer (`errorRetryTimer`, 5s) than the existing wrong-password one (`retryTimer`, 600ms) — reusing the fast one would mean an unattended config problem calls `PamContext.start()` roughly every 600ms indefinitely, real risk of `pam_faillock`-style modules locking the account itself on a real `system-auth` stack, which would have turned a config mistake into a second, worse incident. `enabled: root.pam.responseRequired` removed entirely — gating usability on PAM's own state made a PAM-side problem indistinguishable from a hung UI; the field is now always interactive, with `Keys.onReturnPressed`'s own existing guard (never forward a response PAM did not request) doing all the work the fail-closed logic actually needs, unchanged. `focus: true` replaced with an explicit `passwordField.forceActiveFocus()` in the surface's own `Component.onCompleted`. **Before this row can be re-verified, the PAM file must actually be installed this time** — `sudo install -Dm644 <phios-dotfiles checkout>/profiles/desktop/system/etc/pam.d/phi-shell-lock /etc/pam.d/phi-shell-lock`, confirmed with `ls -l` — and the recovery command should be within reach before testing lock again, not looked up after. |
| S-35 | Window overview | awaiting-verification | phi-shell 0ea46eb · dotfiles (this commit) | 2026-09-08 | `Overview/Overview.qml`: a `GridView` of **every** open window across **every** monitor, not just the current workspace (the card's own explicit requirement) — backed by a **new** `Services/ToplevelBridge.qml`, a thin wrapper over `Quickshell.Wayland`'s `ToplevelManager` (the `wlr-foreign-toplevel-management` protocol, cross-compositor — the same choice `S-33`'s Go-side `WindowsProvider` made independently for `phi query`'s own window results, and documented there for the same reason: real `activate()`/`close()` per window, no hand-rolled `hyprctl dispatch` string, and this surface stays correct even if `HyprlandBridge`'s own API ever moves). Icon+title only (`S-35 AGENT`: "start with icon+title. Live previews are an upgrade, not a requirement") — confirmed against `Quickshell.Wayland.ScreencopyView`'s real capability (checked early in this session, before `S-30`) that it renders a live feed into the scene rather than encoding a file, so a genuine live-preview grid needs per-window screencopy sources this step has no grounds to build for an explicitly deferred upgrade. No text search, per the card's own instruction — `GridView`'s built-in keyboard arrow navigation plus Enter/click. **Two real bugs found and fixed before committing:** (1) a `root.currentIndex` meant to mirror `GridView`'s own `currentIndex` both ways (`currentIndex: root.currentIndex` plus a change handler writing back) was the **same broken-binding class already found twice this milestone** (`S-33`'s search field, and the `Widgets/Panel` `contentItem`-indirection bug `S-31`/`S-32` found) — `GridView`'s own built-in arrow-key handling assigns to `currentIndex` imperatively, which breaks an incoming binding the moment it first happens, so resetting the selection on reopen would have silently stopped working after the very first navigation; fixed by **removing the redundant property entirely** (`grid` now owns `currentIndex` by itself) rather than routing around the hazard a third time. (2) The grid cell's icon+title layout repeated the exact `Widgets.Panel` `contentItem`-indirection bug (a nested child's real `parent` is Panel's inner `contentItem`, not the Panel itself) `S-31`/`S-32` already found and fixed — caught here before commit by now actively checking every `parent.width`/`parent.padding` reference against that pattern as a matter of course, not by surprise. **A third, process-level catch, also before commit:** the first draft of this file reached `Quickshell.Wayland`'s `ToplevelManager` directly and set `WlrLayershell.layer: WlrLayer.Overlay`, both violating `phi-shell/CLAUDE.md`'s own rule that only `Config/` and files under `Services/` may touch that surface — fixed by routing window data through the new `Services/ToplevelBridge.qml` (exactly the pattern this repo already established for every other service surface) and by **dropping the layer override entirely**, since no other surface in this repo (`Bar`, `Toast`, `Sidebar`, `Launcher`) sets one either, all correctly relying on the real documented default (`Top`) rather than importing `Quickshell.Wayland` in a non-`Services/` file just to ask for a different one. **Recall gesture** (`S-35 AGENT`: "three-finger swipe up/down... native Hyprland on razer's touchpad"): added to `hyprland.lua` in this same step — three-finger swipe **up opens, down closes** (a common show/dismiss convention, not both directions toggling the same thing), the opposite axis to Hyprland's own built-in workspace-swipe left/right, avoiding the conflict the card's own wording flags. **Syntax verified against the real Hyprland wiki source before writing it** (`hyprwm/hyprland-wiki`, `content/configuring/core/binds/gestures.md` — not the URL a web search first suggested, which 404'd; found by walking the repo's own directory tree instead) — `hl.gesture({ fingers = 3, direction = "up"/"down", action = function() ... end })`, matching the Lua config API this file already uses throughout (`hl.bind`, `hl.window_rule`), not a different or older classic-syntax form. Inert on `zotac` by construction, no host-specific guard needed: gestures need touchpad hardware that machine does not have, the same capability-driven default (ADR 074) already used everywhere else in this project rather than branching on hostname. **On `zotac`, the card's own alternative — Solaar rules covering the MX Master's own gesture button — is explicitly `S-43`'s job, not this step's; not investigated here.** Single instance on `Quickshell.screens[0]`, same simplification `Sidebar`/`Launcher` already document — the overlay's on-screen *position* is one output, its *content* still lists windows from every monitor, which is what the card's own `VERIFY` line actually asks for. **Not verified, cannot be off-machine:** whether the gesture actually fires on real touchpad hardware (this agent has never touched a touchpad); whether `GridView`'s layout looks right with a real, unpredictable number of open windows; whether `DesktopEntries.heuristicLookup(appId)` reliably resolves real installed apps' icons — same conservative text-only fallback already used at `S-30`/`S-31` for exactly this uncertainty. **Correction, found on real hardware during the M3 VERIFY round, phi-shell commit `fb79ed8`:** `Overview.qml`'s root-level `Behavior on opacity` was written against a property `PanelWindow` does not have — see `S-30`'s row for the full explanation and fix. Applied the same `Services/LayerFocus.qml` fix as `S-33`'s row (needed here for `GridView`'s own arrow-key navigation and `Keys.onEscapePressed`/`onReturnPressed`), but this is explicitly **not assumed to fix the click/activate issue** — pointer/click events are not gated by keyboard focus mode, so that finding needs a retest after this fix rather than being treated as already resolved; if it persists, `Launcher.qml`'s own `activateWindow` action (`hyprctl dispatch focuswindow address:...`) is an already-proven fallback mechanism in this repo. **Correction to S-37's row (see there): activation via Enter/Return DOES correctly focus the selected window here** — confirmed by the user directly, in the same message that reported `AltTab.qml`'s own Return-confirm does not. | S-36 | Screenshot, OCR, QR, recording | awaiting-verification | phi-shell 0e74666 (+ four respawn-bug correction commits, see S-13/S-20/S-23/S-30 rows) · dotfiles 8236e50 | 2026-09-08 | `Screenshot/Screenshot.qml`: capture built in-house (ADR 075), `grim` doing the actual pixel capture (the standard wlroots-ecosystem backend), `tesseract`/`zbarimg` (already declared, `S-15`'s base profile) for OCR/QR, `wf-recorder` for video. **`Quickshell.Wayland.ScreencopyView` was already confirmed — early in this session, before `S-30` — to render a live feed rather than encode a file**, exactly the verification this step's own card asks for ("first VERIFY whether Quickshell's declared screen-recording integration covers full encoding or only frame capture; do not discard `wf-recorder` before checking") — done before committing to `grim`/`wf-recorder`, not after. Selection is a QML drag-rectangle overlay, **not `slurp`'s own interactive picker** — "selection overlay built in QML" (the card's own words) is this file's replacement for exactly that role, so `slurp` (added to `profiles/desktop/packages.txt` anyway, for fidelity to master plan §15.3's own `grim`+`slurp` pairing, in case a user wants it for something else) is deliberately never invoked — flagged for cheap veto as an intentional deviation from the registry row's literal implication. All three region-based modes (save/OCR/QR) share one drag-select overlay; fullscreen and window capture need none (window mode reads `hyprctl activewindow -j` directly, the one place this file touches Hyprland state outside `Services/HyprlandBridge.qml`, since that wrapper exposes no pixel geometry at all — confirmed against the real header during `S-33`/`S-35`'s own research). Recording scope is the focused output, full-frame, not an arbitrary region — an explicit, disclosed simplification (`wf-recorder`'s own `-g` flag would make an area-recording mode a small addition later, not built now, since the card asks only for "invoked... for encoding only"). **`wf-recorder` needs `SIGINT`, not `SIGTERM`, to finalise its output file correctly** (this tool's own well-established behaviour, not re-verified against its source this session) — `Quickshell`'s own `Process.running = false` sends `SIGTERM` (confirmed against the real source, `io/process.cpp`: `setRunning(false)` calls `QProcess::terminate()`), so stopping a recording sends `SIGINT` explicitly via the process's own `processId`, not through that path. **One design bug caught and fixed before committing:** an early draft routed window-mode capture through the same `mode` property the text-selection modes use, with a `onModeChanged` handler that immediately reset it again in the same JS tick — this would likely have worked (same-tick set-then-reset never actually gets rendered), but relied on that timing subtlety for no reason; simplified to call the `hyprctl` query directly from the IPC handler instead, no `mode`/`onModeChanged` involved at all for that path. **The step that found and fixed a real, systemic bug across the whole `phi-shell` repository, not just this file — recorded in full on each affected step's own row (`S-13`, `S-20`, `S-23`, and a second correction on `S-30`), summarized here as the trigger:** this file's own `mkdir -p` safety `Process` was first written with `running: true` and no `onExited` reset, which would have respawned it forever the moment it first completed. Catching that prompted auditing **every** `Process` in the entire repository for the same mistake — read directly from the real Quickshell source (`io/process.cpp`): `Process::onFinished()` calls `startProcessIfReady()` **unconditionally** as its last step, so any one-shot `Process` left with `running` still `true` after it exits respawns itself immediately, in a tight loop, forever — `destroy()` alone does not prevent this, since it only schedules deferred deletion of a still-fully-alive object. Four **already-shipped, already-"verified"** instances were found and fixed this same session: `Config/Settings.qml` (`S-13` — every `phi state get/set/list` call in this shell has gone through this path since then), `Config/Capabilities.qml` (`S-20` — **the single most severe instance**: this probe has been respawning in a tight, uninterrupted loop from shell startup, on every real session run to date, not periodically as intended), `Services/Tailscale.qml` and `Bar/modules/Gpu.qml` (`S-23` — both pollers were completely decoupled from their own `Timer`s' intended intervals), and `Services/Notifications.qml`'s own `mkdir` process (a second, independent defect on top of the `trackedNotifications` leak `S-30`'s first correction already fixed). **This is a real, load-bearing finding that reopens confidence in "verified" as a status label**, not just a note: `S-13`, `S-20`, and `S-23` were all marked `verified` — some after real-hardware confirmation of their own stated `DONE WHEN` — while carrying this defect the whole time, because the respawn loop produces no VISIBLE symptom a manual functional test would catch (the polled values still update, just via a tight loop instead of the intended cadence) — only reading the actual mechanism against the real Quickshell source surfaced it. Real-hardware CPU/battery impact from any of the four pre-existing instances was never measured before these fixes and remains unconfirmed — only the mechanism is confirmed, not its practical severity on `zotac`/`razer` to date — flagged prominently for the final question list rather than asserted either way. Package additions: `grim`, `slurp`, `wf-recorder`, `zbar` — all four pre-approved, master plan §15.3, milestone M3. **Not verified, cannot be off-machine:** the entire capture/OCR/QR/recording pipeline end to end — nothing here has run inside a real Quickshell process, on real Wayland, against real `grim`/`tesseract`/`zbarimg`/`wf-recorder` binaries; whether the drag-select overlay's coordinate math (`screen.x`/`screen.y` plus local mouse position) produces a geometry string `grim` accepts without a sign or off-by-one error; whether `wl-copy --type image/png` piped from a file behaves as expected. **Real-hardware VERIFY feedback:** window and fullscreen capture work; area selection (and, downstream of it, OCR and QR, which reuse the same overlay) is offset "to the top by a lot" — the exact coordinate bug this row's own header already flagged as unverified, now confirmed real rather than fixed, since it needs `razer`'s actual scale factor (`hyprctl monitors -j`) to diagnose correctly rather than guessed at; held for that data rather than patched blind. **Recording, phi-shell commit `56c6b4f`:** the stopped file could not be opened (`mpv`: "moov atom not found," the standard symptom of a video file whose trailer was never written because the encoder did not exit cleanly). `_stopRecording()` spawned a separate `kill -INT <pid>` `Process` using `recordProc.processId`; replaced with `recordProc.signal(2)`, a real, confirmed `Process` `Q_INVOKABLE` method (`io/process.hpp`) that sends the signal directly with no separate process or `processId` timing dependency. Whether the old indirection was the actual cause of the corrupted file is not confirmed, but the direct method removes that entire failure surface regardless. **Second real-hardware round: the area-selection offset is fixed, recording is not.** Confirmed the offset's actual cause from `hyprctl monitors -j` data (razer: `"scale": 2`): `root.screen.x/y` and mouse coordinates are device-independent (logical) pixels — confirmed against the real source, `src/core/qmlscreen.hpp`, `ShellScreen` wraps a plain `QScreen` and reads its geometry directly, standard Qt platform convention — but `grim -g` wants physical compositor pixels, the same space `hyprctl monitors -j` itself reports `width`/`height` in. Window and fullscreen capture never hit this since neither derives its geometry from QML coordinates at all. Fixed (`phi-shell e63f44e`) by multiplying by `root.screen.devicePixelRatio`. **Recording is still broken, and worse than first reported:** the `Process.signal(2)` fix from the previous round did not resolve it — `Shift+R` does not stop the recording, and neither does terminating `qs` itself; the file keeps growing until the user kills `qs` directly. A `wf-recorder` that survives `qs` terminating is not being tracked as `qs`'s own child process in any way that termination reaches it. Held rather than guessed at a third time — the discriminating test (`qs -p ~/.config/quickshell/phi ipc call record stop` directly, bypassing the `Shift+R` keybind) is requested from the user before touching this again. **Root cause found (dotfiles `ed947e7`):** the user's test confirmed the direct `qs ipc call record stop` *does* stop the recording correctly — `Screenshot.qml`'s own handler was never the problem. `r` (start) used `fireAndReset()`, the same helper every other one-shot screenshot action uses, which exits the `screenshot` submap immediately after firing — but recording spans time, so `SHIFT + r` (bound only inside that submap) was unreachable the moment recording started, unless the user pressed `Print` again first. `r` no longer resets; only `Shift + r` does. **Fourth real-hardware round: marked broken and deferred, per the user's own explicit instruction, not investigated further.** `Shift + r` still does not stop the recording — the user's own words, "it just types shift+R in the active window" — meaning the fix above did not resolve the actual symptom on real hardware, for a reason this project has not further diagnosed. The user's own explicit redirect: the *intended* interaction was never meant to be a keybind at all — a stop button on the bar is the right design, out of scope for this session — so this is recorded here as a known, accepted limitation rather than chased a third time. **User confusion flagged for future clarity:** a prior round of this project's own guidance referred to this bind as "Start+Shift+R," which the user did not recognize; that was very likely a garbled way of saying "press Print first, then Shift+R" (Print enters the `screenshot` submap `Shift+R` only exists inside) — future instructions involving a submap-gated bind should say the two-step shape explicitly rather than name the inner key alone. **Follow-up, not built:** a stop button in `Bar/` (its own future step) is the design the user wants; the `record` IPC target's existing `stop` function is exactly what such a button would call, no new backend surface needed. **Fourth real-hardware round: the `devicePixelRatio` area-selection fix (`e63f44e`) is still broken.** The user confirmed the `phi-shell` checkout on the affected machine is up to date (ruling out, at least for this retest, the stale-checkout explanation raised for the same-round `AltTab.qml` finding below) — the offset persists despite that. No exact symptom (still offset "to the top by a lot," offset in a new way, or something else) was re-described this round; not re-diagnosed further this turn, only recorded as still-broken per the user's own explicit instruction to write it down rather than guess again. Combined with the `AltTab.qml` findings on the same round (S-37's row), this is now the **second independent, source-grounded fix producing no observable change on a confirmed-current checkout** — raises the same question S-37's row raises about whether the running `qs` process is actually the one that loaded this code (a stale *running process*, as opposed to a stale checkout — `qs` does not restart itself just because a `git pull` happened, only `Quickshell`'s own file-watch hot-reload or an explicit `pkill -x qs && qs -p ...` picks up a change) rather than the fix itself being wrong. Held for that data rather than attempting a third area-selection mechanism blind. |

Window data via `Services/ToplevelBridge.qml` (`S-35`), so the two window-listing surfaces in this shell can never disagree about what windows exist. Selection starts at index 1 (not 0) when more than one window is open, matching the usual "jump straight to the other window" convention — **`ToplevelManager`'s own ordering is unconfirmed** (no document states whether it is creation order, activation order, or something else), flagged for cheap veto if that reads wrong on real hardware; the cycling mechanism itself does not depend on the order being any particular one. **`Tooltip/Tooltip.qml`:** deliberately **not** built on `Quickshell.PopupWindow` — a same-window floating widget positioned via plain `Item.mapToItem` coordinate mapping instead, since every plausible tooltip consumer (a bar segment, a sidebar row) already lives in the same window it would show one inside, so there is no reason to take on `PopupWindow`'s cross-window anchoring risk just for this. (3) the cheat sheet showed raw internal values like `"_lua 16"` instead of anything readable — a real, documented `description` bind flag exists (`flags.md`) and is now set on every phi-shell bind; `Cheatsheet.qml`'s own `_describe()` already preferred `bind.description` first, it simply never had one to read. **Second real-hardware round:** Alt+Tab now shows the overlay on the first press (fixed); releasing Alt still neither confirms nor closes it — the `ALT_R` addition did not resolve it either, so this remains open, not yet diagnosed further. Every top-level bind now shows its real `description` in the cheat sheet as intended; a few submap-internal entries (`ALT_L`/`ALT_R`, the escape/catchall cancel binds) were left undescribed and still show `"_lua 36"`-style raw values, and `Escape` appears twice (once per submap it's bound in) since `hyprctl binds -j` lists every registered bind across every submap, not just the currently active one. **User request, not yet built:** some cheat sheet rows should be hideable, "in a manner that's easy to change in future" — read as a request for `Cheatsheet.qml`'s own filtering to be data-driven (e.g., an exclusion list or a `hidden` convention) rather than hardcoded conditionals; acknowledged, deferred to avoid widening this correction round further, needs its own small pass. **Third real-hardware round, Alt+Tab release confirmed NOT a bug in this file (dotfiles `ed947e7`):** `hyprwm/Hyprland#15785` on Hyprland's own real issue tracker (closed by tracker policy — GitHub Issues are no longer accepted upstream, redirected to Discussions — not because it was fixed) describes the identical scenario: a modifier held continuously from *before* a submap is entered does not fire its own release bind on its first release, only on a subsequent full press-and-release cycle already inside the submap. Alt+Tab enters this submap with Alt already held, exactly that case — this is a real Hyprland limitation, not something fixable in this project's own code. `ALT_L`/`ALT_R` stay bound (may still fire on some interaction patterns per the same report) but `Return` is added as the reliable confirm path, a fresh press that does not hit this limitation. **Also confirmed on this same round: `windows[selectedIndex].activate()` not actually focusing the target window is not surface-specific** — the user's report ("it never select the active panel") describes the identical symptom already found on `S-35`'s `Overview.qml`, both calling the same `Toplevel.activate()` method from the same `Services/ToplevelBridge.qml`. This strengthens the case that it is a real `Toplevel.activate()` limitation rather than something wrong in either surface's own code, and the fix both rows independently flagged — falling back to `hyprctl dispatch focuswindow address:...`, the mechanism `Launcher.qml`'s own `activateWindow` action already proves works — needs the user's own call before building it: the cross-compositor `ToplevelBridge` deliberately does not expose a Hyprland-specific `address` field (that lives on the separate `HyprlandToplevel` wrapper), so correlating the two would mean either switching this surface's window source to the Hyprland-specific one (trading the documented cross-compositor design goal for reliability) or matching entries across the two by title/appId (fragile). Held for that decision rather than guessed at. **Fourth real-hardware round (phi-shell/dotfiles, this commit), addressing the user's two explicit directives — fix Alt+Tab's selection, fix confirmation on Alt release — and note, not build, the Overview findings:** (1) **Selection was actually two separate bugs, both fixed.** Activation: matching the prior row's held decision, now built — a fresh one-off `hyprctl clients -j` query at confirm time, matched by `title`+`class` (confirmed field names, cross-checked against phi's own Go code, `internal/query/windows.go`), dispatching `hyprctl dispatch focuswindow address:...`, the same mechanism `Launcher.qml`'s own `activateWindow` action already proves works here — chosen over switching this surface's own window source, since only the one-shot activation step needs Hyprland-specific data, not the live cycling/highlighting. Starting position: the user's own description of Overview's bug ("always starts at the first element... rather then the active one") turned out to describe Alt+Tab too — the earlier `index 1` starting guess assumed `ToplevelManager` orders its list active-first, flagged here as unconfirmed at the time and now understood to be the actual defect. Fixed by querying `hyprctl activewindow -j` on entry (same Client JSON shape as `clients -j`) and selecting the window *after* whichever one it reports as focused, instead of trusting list order; `selectedIndex` is set provisionally to `1` the instant the overlay opens so it is never left unselected while that query is in flight, then corrected once it returns. (2) **Confirmation on Alt release: a materially different mechanism was tried, not yet confirmed on real hardware.** The `#15785` finding stands — a modifier held from before a submap is entered does not fire its own release bind on first release *inside that submap*. Re-read `flags.md` directly (not from memory this time) and found `submap_universal` ("will be active no matter the submap") is real. Moved the `ALT_L`/`ALT_R` release-confirm bind out of `hl.define_submap("alttab", ...)` to the global keymap with `submap_universal = true` — registered once, before Alt+Tab is ever pressed, which is not the scenario `#15785` describes. `AltTab.qml`'s own `confirm()` IPC function is now guarded on `root.shown`, since a global release bind can fire from any Alt release unrelated to Alt+Tab (e.g. AltGr on some layouts is the physical right-Alt key) and must be a safe no-op there. `Return` stays as the always-reliable fallback regardless of whether this fires. **Not yet re-tested on real hardware** — a better-targeted attempt than the earlier `ALT_R` addition that did not work, not a guaranteed fix. Preventive snapshot via `snapper -c root create` — `"root"` is this project's own installation procedures' own config name, not re-verified against a live snapper config from here, degrades to a non-fatal warning if wrong. - **openrazer-daemon fails to start — root cause confirmed, fixed the only way this agent can.** The user pasted the untruncated CRITICAL log: `User is not a member of the openrazer group. Please run the command 'sudo gpasswd -a $USER openrazer' and then reboot!`. Not an environment/compatibility problem after all (round 3's own working guess) — a plain missing supplementary group. New `profiles/razer-hw/manual.txt` (`sudo gpasswd -a $USER openrazer`, plus the comment explaining a reboot or fresh login is still needed afterward): the same "declare it, never apply it" contract every other manual step in this repo already uses for an `/etc`-adjacent, one-off, `sudo`-requiring account change (`base/manual.txt`'s `pacman-key --add`, `gaming/manual.txt`'s multilib `sed`). `bin/lib/common.sh`'s `phios_read_list` confirmed to parse the new file cleanly off-machine; `razer-hw` is declared for `razer` in `hosts/razer.txt`, so `phios-install`'s manual-steps report will surface it. Chroma (S-46) and the user's own `python-openrazer` enumeration (Q-F04/Q-F06) both depend on this and stay blocked until the user runs it and reboots.
- **Spotlight position — real root cause found, not guessed a third time.** `Spotlight/Spotlight.qml`'s `PanelWindow` left `exclusionMode` at its default (`Auto`), which Quickshell's own docs define a shrink-to-content behaviour for ONLY when exactly three anchors are connected; this window anchors all four, so it fell back to respecting other layers' exclusive zones, same as `Normal` would. `Bar/Bar.qml` reserves `bar.height` at the top whenever not auto-hidden, so the window's real on-screen top-left sat `bar.height` below `screen.y` while the cursor math still subtracted bare `screen.y` — every local Y came out too large by exactly the bar's height, drawing the vignette too far down. Matches the reported symptom exactly, and explains why neither round 1 (scale conversion) nor round 2 (revert) touched it: both only ever changed the cursor sample, never the window's own placement. Fixed with `exclusionMode: ExclusionMode.Ignore` (Quickshell docs, `Quickshell/ExclusionMode`: "Ignore exclusion zones of other shell layers"), confirmed via the real Quickshell documentation before writing, not assumed. **The pre-existing `exclusiveZone: 0` line was removed, not kept alongside it**: the same docs state that assigning `exclusiveZone` sets `exclusionMode` back to `Normal` as a side effect, confirmed before committing — keeping both would have silently undone this fix and produced an identical-looking "still slightly down" with no way to tell the fix never took effect. **Still unverified end to end — no compositor here.**
- **Spotlight interaction model — the user's own decision, not guessed.** Double-click-and-hold Super, replacing SUPER+G hold. `hyprland.lua` now binds the bare `SUPER_L` keysym itself (press and release, no combo) instead of `SUPER + G`/bare-`g` — mirrors the bare-key-release shape this file's own Alt+Tab binds (`ALT_L`/`ALT_R`) and the prior spotlight round already established, to avoid the same "released before the other key" class of bug. The double-click timing is new state in `Services/Spotlight.qml` (`press()`/`release()`, 400ms threshold, owns `_lastRelease`/`_armed`), not in `hyprland.lua`, keeping that file a thin dispatcher as it is everywhere else. **`non_consuming = true` added to both binds as a safety measure**: no existing bind in this file puts a PRESS bind on a bare modifier keysym at the top level (ALT_L/ALT_R are release-only and scoped to the alttab submap), so whether an unflagged press bind would suppress the SUPER modmask for every `SUPER + <key>` combo below (Return, B, E, Q, arrows, Space/launcher, N, S, L, Tab, Shift+slash, workspaces) could not be confirmed from here — the flag costs nothing if the fear is unfounded, and the alternative (a dead primary modifier on the user's own machine, discovered after a handoff) is not recoverable from here. **VERIFY below checks the SUPER + <key> binds before the spotlight itself, on purpose.** **Known, flagged, not fixed limitation**: bare `SUPER_L` fires on every plain Super press/release, including the start/end of ordinary `SUPER + <key>` combos — two such combos used back to back within 400ms could be misread as the qualifying double click. Guarding against that would require every existing `SUPER + X` bind in the file to signal "a combo happened", out of scope for this fix. `Settings/sections/Theme.qml`'s toggle-row label updated to match. **Unverified on real hardware.**
- **Spotlight cursor marker**: already removed, round 3 (`phi-shell` b69eaa1) — restated here only because the user's own message repeated the ask; no further change needed.

- **Spotlight gesture: confirmed broken on real hardware, not fixed this round.** The user tried the round-4 double-click-and-hold and nothing happened; they turned it on via the Settings panel toggle instead (to test the position fix, which is how they confirmed it), and then the keybind also could not turn it back off, leaving the overlay stuck on — recorded here as a real, self-inflicted design gap in `Services/Spotlight.qml`'s own state machine, not only a Hyprland problem: `release()` only called `hide()` when `_armed` was true, so a `shown` set by the settings toggle (which bypasses the tap-count state entirely) could only ever be cleared by a full, *subsequent* qualifying tap-and-release cycle — worth understanding even though it does not fully explain what the user saw, since a genuine qualifying cycle should still have reached `hide()` regardless of how `shown` became true. **Immediate unstick given to the user**: `qs -p ~/.config/quickshell/phi ipc call spotlight toggle` — calls the low-level `toggle()` directly, bypassing the broken gesture path entirely. Real research this round, not another guess: fetched the actual hyprland-wiki Lua-binding docs (`content/configuring/core/binds/_index.md`, `flags.md`) and Hyprland's own `src/keybinds/Key.cpp` and test fixtures (`tests/keybinds/Bind.cpp`) from `github.com/hyprwm/Hyprland` directly, not community `hyprland.conf` forum posts (which use a different, older config format's `bindr=MOD,MOD_L,...` idiom that does not apply to this repo's Lua `hl.bind()` config) — confirmed real: sided-modifier-only binds are a genuine, tested engine feature (`CKey`'s `SSidedModifierPattern`), keysym name matching is case-insensitive (`XKB_KEYSYM_CASE_INSENSITIVE` in `Key.cpp`), and `flags.md` carries a dedicated worked example for exactly this shape: `hl.bind("SUPER_L", hl.dsp.exec_cmd("pkill wofi || wofi"))`, **no flags at all**. Round 4's press bind differed from that documented example by exactly one thing — `non_consuming = true`, added last round from an unconfirmed worry about combo breakage — removed from both the press and release binds this round as the one concrete, checkable difference, **not asserted as the actual fix**: this agent has now been wrong twice about spotlight's real cause (round 2's marker, round 4's untested `non_consuming` worry) and is not making a third unverified claim. The real discriminator, asked of the user rather than guessed: whether Hyprland's own bind registry carries this bind at all (`hyprctl binds -j` filtered for `SUPER_L`, plus `hyprctl version`, both in this round's `VERIFY`) — that result decides whether the next round is a syntax/version fix or something else entirely, instead of a sixth blind attempt. Also changed, by the user's own explicit request and independent of whether the bind fires yet: the required tap count generalised from a hardcoded double-click to `Services/Spotlight.qml`'s `requiredTaps` property, set to 3 (triple-click) — a one-line change if it needs to move again. **Not confirmed whether "restarted hyprland" meant a full session restart or `hyprctl reload`** — asked the user directly this round rather than assumed, since it changes which stale-config explanations are even possible.

**ROUND 6 — real root cause found: this is a Hyprland compositor bug, not fixable from any config in this repo. No code changed this round; asked the user to choose a substitute, per this repo's own "stop and ask" rule rather than a sixth blind rebind.**

- **`hyprctl binds -j` (user-provided) confirms both binds ARE registered correctly**: `key: 'SUPER_L'`, `modmask: 0`, one with `release: False` (press) carrying the real description, one with `release: True`, both `dispatcher: '__lua'` — exactly the shape round 5's Lua wrote. This rules out every "wrong syntax" / "wrong Hyprland version for this feature" theory from round 5: the compositor accepted and stored the binds exactly as intended.
- **The user's own comparison is the key datum: "it also used to work with super+G. It stopped working since it was set as double tap and release."** SUPER+G (rounds 1–3) is a bind on a REAL key (G) with SUPER as an ordinary held modifier — both its press and its release are ordinary, non-modifier key events, reliably delivered. The regression traces to the one thing that changed: binding press AND release on the bare modifier keysym itself (`SUPER_L`), not to anything in this repo's Lua or in phi-shell's IPC/state handling, both of which are the same mechanism that already worked for SUPER+G.
- **Root cause, found by searching Hyprland's own issue tracker rather than guessing a sixth variant**: `github.com/hyprwm/Hyprland` issue #6946, "Bind super alone on release no longer works". Opened 2024, closed 2024-07-25 — but its most recent comment, dated 2026-08-17, reports it **still reproduces on Hyprland 0.56.2**, with an actual diagnostic: a logging build across 11 candidate keycodes recorded 7 presses and **zero releases**, across every binding variant tried (plain keysym, `mods=SUPER key=SUPER_L`, raw keycode). The reporter's own conclusion: "this does look like a compositor-level dispatch gap for modifier-key release events specifically, not something fixable from userspace config." This matches this session's own evidence exactly: press plausibly fires (consistent with the JSON showing the bind registered and the described one being the press bind), release never does — which is also why `Services/Spotlight.qml`'s tap counter, which can only advance a streak once `_lastRelease` is set by a real `release()` call, never armed at all: with no release ever delivered, `_lastRelease` stays `0` forever, `continuing` in `press()` is always `false`, and `show()` is never reached by the gesture path. This is a complete, sourced explanation for both symptoms reported since round 4 — the double-tap never showing anything, and the settings-toggled overlay never being dismissable by the keybind — with nothing left unexplained.
- **Not fixable from any file in this repository.** Every syntax variant this bug report's commenters tried (including the exact ones round 4 and round 5 used) fails the same way; this is inside the compositor's input dispatch, upstream of `hyprland.lua`, `hl.bind()`, and phi-shell entirely.
- **Asked the user to choose a substitute** rather than guess one: revert to plain hold-on-SUPER+G (simplest, already proven reliable, drops the tap-count entirely), keep the tap-count novelty but move it onto SUPER+G (multi-tap-and-hold on a real key instead of bare Super, since G's own press/release are both reliable), or something else. **User chose plain hold-on-SUPER+G**, dropping the tap-count idea entirely rather than moving it onto a real key. `hyprland.lua`'s bare-`SUPER_L` press/release pair is reverted to the exact round-2/3 shape (`mainMod + G` press, bare-`g` release with `non_consuming`), and `Services/Spotlight.qml`'s tap-count state machine (`requiredTaps`/`tapWindowMs`/`_lastRelease`/`_completedTaps`/`_armed`, `press()`/`release()`) is deleted outright rather than left unused — `shell.qml`'s `IpcHandler` calls `show()`/`hide()` directly again, and `Settings/sections/Theme.qml`'s label reverted to match. This closes the interaction-model question for good: the underlying platform bug rules out any bare-modifier-alone gesture, not just this session's two attempts at one.

| Step | Title | Status | Commit | Date | Note |

| S-50 | Final palette, derived in OKLCH | awaiting-verification | dotfiles (this commit) | 2026-09-09 | Real OKLCH derivation, not a hand-picked adjustment: every changed value is a fixed OKLCH hue/chroma anchor with lightness found by bisection against `phi`'s own WCAG contrast formula (`phi/internal/tokens/color.go`), gamut-checked so no solve ever silently clips a hue. `phi theme check` (already built, M1) now reports **zero violations on both variants** — built and run locally against this checkout to confirm, not asserted (`PHI_DOTFILES=<checkout> phi theme check`). Three real fixes: (1) **dark fg-2's 4.00:1 failure, closed** — re-solved the fg-0..fg-3 ramp at fg-2's own hue (H=82.39°) for a real 4.6:1 target instead of forcing fg-2 through fg-3's old anchor; fg-1/fg-3 fall out as the remaining two points of a still-uniform 4-point ramp. Light's fg-2 (already passing at 4.58:1) re-solved the same way, same 4.6:1 target, for one documented algorithm instead of an ad hoc affine map on one side only. (2) **Tier 3 syntax given real derived values**, not literal aliases of Tier 1/2 — same hue per role, lightness solved for a shared syntax-legibility target (5.6:1 dark) distinct from each role's own individual contrast. **Caught and fixed before commit**: the first light-mode pass used the same 4.9:1 target as Tier 2's own light re-solve, which at identical hue/chroma converges on the identical lightness — warn/error/info/type/number/function would have collided pixel-for-pixel with their Tier 2 counterpart.
